# TECH_DEBT.md — Vela Pre-Existing Gaps & Polish Items

Active tech debt entries identified during shipping. Format preserved verbose because each entry is dense diagnosis context — compressing to a table loses why/how-to-apply specificity.

<!-- NAVIGATION TABLE — added 2026-08-11. Navigation only; no entry text was changed. -->
## Navigation — by class

**This file's convention: entries are MARKED, never deleted.** A `[DONE]` entry is retained as the record of something that happened, not as work.
Classes are **additive and orthogonal** to the existing `[P0]`–`[P3]` ratings — a class says *what kind of obligation* an entry is, a rating says *how urgent*.

| class | meaning | count |
|---|---|---|
| **[LAUNCH]** | blocks the B2B-interim route going live to a real customer | **0 — EMPTY** |
| **[COMPLIANCE]** | legal / regulatory / data-protection obligation | **8** |
| **[HONESTY]** | the product currently tells the user something untrue or misleading | **20** |
| [DONE] | already fixed / resolved / accepted; retained for the record only | 49 |
| [OTHER] | quality, hygiene, tooling, opportunistic | 89 |
| | **total** | **166** |

- [DONE] **[P2 · /history render fidelity — surfaced by the founder's fly-241 prod pass 2026-09-01; DERIVED per mode; ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-03 — fly 242 (Verify + Research half A) + fly 244 (Research half B): all three modes now store the full structure and render through shared components; old rows stay Verify summary-only / Research half A forever (unbackfillable)] History fidelity is three different shapes: Explain FULL (the target), Research raw-markdown (text stored, renderer missing), Verify summary-only (detail NEVER stored — unbackfillable)**
  - **Founder direction for the eventual fix, recorded:** Explain's history fidelity is the TARGET EXPERIENCE for all three modes.
  - **Explain = shape (i), fully realized — this is why it looks right:** the write path stores the FULL structured result as JSON serialized into the Text column (`server.py:1576-1593`, `answer=json.dumps(event content)`), and `pages/history.tsx:219-268` safe-parses it and renders the real `ExplainItemCard` + `ClinicalCorrelationCard` with a legacy plain-text fallback. That renderer was itself a deliberate fix (`fbad02c` *"render Explain records as cards, not raw JSON"*) — the other two modes never received their equivalent.
  - **Research = shape (iii), two halves with different costs:** the write path stores the COMPLETE answer markdown (`full_answer` accumulates every SSE answer chunk, `server.py:899`, stored at `:932`) — but `history.tsx:203-217` renders it as a plain `<p whitespace-pre-wrap>` inside a `prose` wrapper, so `## Summary`, `---` and `[4]` display as raw text. **(half A, frontend-only):** markdown rendering of sections/lists — the section format's established consumers already exist (CLAUDE.md Rule 12: `api/rag/generator.py:358-375` is source of truth; `share_renderer.parse_research_sections` + `pages/research.tsx` parse it), so history can reuse the research page's own rendering. **(half B, write-path + NO backfill):** the CITATION LIST is never stored — `citations_data` streams as a separate SSE event (`server.py:~905`) into `CitationLog`, not into `ChatHistory`; and per the 2026-08-18 scoping there is **no correlation key between `chat_history` and anything else** (zero FKs, no request id), so old rows' bare `[N]` markers can be styled but can never resolve to their references. Full Explain-parity for Research needs the write path to persist citations alongside the answer; **existing rows cannot be backfilled.**
  - **Verify = shape (ii), flatly:** all three INSERT sites store ONLY the summary line — `answer=summary` (`server.py:1473`), `fb_summary` (`:1324`), `fallback_summary` (`:1366`). The per-interaction detail (drug pairs, severities, per-finding source attribution) is NEVER persisted, which is why prod shows only *"Found 1 interaction(s): 1 Major"*. Fix = write-path change (persist the `VerifyResponse` structure the way Explain persists its result) + a history renderer reusing the verify page's cards; **old rows cannot be backfilled — the detail was never written and no correlation key exists to recover it from any other table.**
  - ⚠️ **Adjacent, pre-existing, NOT this entry:** the `[COMPLIANCE][P2]` *"`ChatHistory.answer` stored unsanitized"* entry — any write-path change here (storing MORE per mode) should be designed with that entry in view, not in ignorance of it. *(2026-09-01: designed with it in view — sanitization declined in-car by founder ruling §6#9; see that entry's dated line.)*

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · /history fallback indication — surfaced 2026-09-02 by the segment-3 Rule 19 carry-across, NOT fixed] /history renders a FALLBACK-generated Research answer (no literature retrieved — LLM knowledge only) IDENTICALLY to a literature-grounded one: the fallback flag is not in the ruled `research_v1` payload, so the clinical caveat /research shows for such answers never reaches history**
  - **Mechanism, derived at HEAD `2dd3f84`:** on /research the caveat is `FallbackBanner` (`pages/research.tsx:102`, strings `ui.noLiteratureFound` + body), rendered when `isFallback` is true (`:616-617`, the page comment calls it the *"floor (keeps the clinical caveat)"*, mutually exclusive with `ProvenanceLine`). `isFallback` is set by the SSE event `type: 'fallback'` (`:430`), which `api/server.py:906-907` emits from `StreamEventType.FALLBACK`, yielded by `api/rag/generator.py:289` (`content="no_literature"`) inside `_generate_fallback_stream` — entered iff `not documents` (`generator.py:169-172`). **None of this is persisted:** `_research_history_payload` (`api/server.py:1072-1076`) stores exactly `{kind, answer, citations}` — no fallback field, per the founder's 2026-09-02 ruling (payload = markdown + the streamed citation list, nothing else). The history renderer (`pages/history.tsx` research branch) therefore shows a fallback answer as section cards with no banner, no provenance line and — because its `citations` is `[]` — no references block: a grounded-looking answer whose ungrounded status has been dropped.
  - **Can /history tell them apart today? NO.** The only in-row proxy, `citations == []`, is (a) not rendered as anything, and (b) NOT unique to fallback: the `retrieval_status == "error"` path (`generator.py:162-165`, ERROR→DONE) and the generator exception path (`:206-211`, ERROR→DONE) also reach the DONE write with no CITATIONS event, so an empty list can equally be an error-path row (the main path emits one citation per document, `:179`, so it never yields `[]` with documents present). Pre-segment-3 plain-markdown rows carry even less.
  - **Class — [HONESTY], reasoned against this file's definition** (*"the product currently tells the user something untrue or misleading"*): the row prints no false sentence, but it misleads by omission in exactly the way /research's own design says matters — the banner exists because an ungrounded clinical answer shown without its caveat reads as grounded; /history now gives grounded rows a references block and ungrounded rows nothing, with no marker that the difference is grounding. A user-facing trust signal dropped on a live page is the honesty test, not a rendering-quality gap. **Rejected: [OTHER]** — the parent fidelity entry is [OTHER] because it concerns missing DETAIL (cards vs a summary line); this one concerns a missing CAVEAT whose absence changes what the user believes about the answer. **P2, not P1:** only fallback-path rows (no-literature queries) are affected and the answer text itself is unchanged.
  - **Fix path (NOT this car — founder ruling 2026-09-02, payload unchanged):** persist the flag — an optional `fallback: bool` on `research_v1` (renderer treats absence as UNKNOWN, never as grounded) or a `research_v2` kind — set from the same `StreamEventType.FALLBACK` branch the SSE event comes from (`server.py:906`, a `event_stream()`-scoped local like `citations_data`, bound at the top for the same error-path reason); then carry `FallbackBanner` + `ProvenanceLine` into the history renderer (both page-local in `pages/research.tsx` today — extraction to `components/`, the `VerifyInteractionCard` move). Strings already exist in all 16 locales (`noLiteratureFound`, `provenanceSourced`) → 0 new i18n keys. **Old rows are unrecoverable:** the flag was never written and no correlation key exists (recon §7-D2).
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-02, segment-3 Rule 19 carry-across (the declined-in-writing item "ProvenanceLine / FallbackBanner"); filed the same day on the founder's follow-up instruction.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · /history share fidelity — surfaced 2026-09-02 by the segment-3 build (recon §4 adjacency), NOT fixed; founder ruling 2026-09-02: NOT this car, follow-up] history's ShareButton passes `citations={[]}` — a public share page created from a research_v1 row shows the markdown with bare `[N]` markers and NO references, although the row now stores the full citation list**
  - **At HEAD `2dd3f84`:** `pages/history.tsx` `<ShareButton … answerText={verifyParsed?.summary ?? researchParsed?.answer ?? item.answer} citations={[]} source="history" />`. The share flow snapshots citations at share time from the client request (`SharedQuery.citations` JSON via `/api/share/create`), so a history-origin research share has ALWAYS published without references — pre-existing since history shares began, recorded in recon §4 (*"history's ShareButton passes `citations={[]}` … research shares created from /history already lose citations"*). Segment 3 makes it fixable for NEW rows only: `researchParsed.citations` is the same `Citation[]` shape the /research page hands `ShareButton`.
  - **Why not fixed in segment 3:** it changes what a PUBLIC page shows (a share created from history would gain a references block) — a scope call, ruled by the founder 2026-09-02 as a follow-up. The recon §10 gate row 7 states the absence is EXPECTED so it is not misread as a segment-3 defect.
  - **Fix path:** pass `researchParsed?.citations ?? []`; before shipping, Rule 19 — list what the /research-origin share does around its citations (the `/api/share/create` PHI gate on `answer_text`, `CitationIn` validation, `share_renderer._augment_citations`) and confirm each is carried or declined for history-origin shares. Old rows: `[]` forever (no citations stored).
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-02, segment 3.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · Research DONE-branch on error — surfaced 2026-09-02 by the segment-3 follow-up (flagged in its build report as pre-existing), filed 2026-09-03, NOT fixed] When the answer generator FAILS, `/api/research` still WRITES a history row and CHARGES 3 credits — the DONE branch never learns that an ERROR preceded it**
  - **Mechanism, derived at HEAD `e5e5688`:** `api/rag/generator.py` emits `ERROR` then `DONE` on three paths — `retrieval_status == "error"` (`:162-165`), the main-path exception (`:206-211`, any provider/model failure) and the fallback-path exception (`:315-318`). `api/server.py` handles `StreamEventType.DONE` (`:923`) without checking for a preceding ERROR, so on every one of those paths it (1) writes `ChatHistory` (`:936-941`, label "History Save") with `answer = _research_history_payload(full_answer, citations_data)` — `full_answer` is `""` or whatever partial text streamed before the exception, citations `[]`; (2) runs `deduct_credits(db, user_id, "research")` (`:943`, 3 credits per `CREDIT_COSTS`, unconditional in TEST_MODE too) — the anon branch likewise runs `deduct_anonymous_credits` (`:927`); (3) skips cost logging (no usage) and the judge (`if audit_id and full_answer`) unless a partial answer streamed. Only a RAISED exception (retrieval raising, a guard throwing) takes the outer `except` (log line *"Research stream error"*), which emits its own error+done WITHOUT the write or the charge — the defect is specific to the generator's own ERROR→DONE convention.
  - **Reachability at HEAD:** the main-path and fallback-path exceptions are LIVE (bad model name, provider 5xx, timeout after retries). The `retrieval_status == "error"` leg is DEAD CODE today — `has_api_error` (`api/rag/retriever.py:211`) is set only when a gathered source task raises, and all five `_search_*` wrappers catch `Exception` and return `[]` (`:600-602`, `:616-618`, `:633-635`, `:663-665`, `:687-689`). Recorded so nobody tries to exercise it without a code change (recon_20260901 §10 row 11).
  - **Two user-facing facets, both untruths:** **(a) billing** — the Navbar meter (`components/Navbar.tsx:281`, "today credits X / limit", fed by `/api/user/status` → `user_usage.credits_used_today`) tells the user they consumed a Research call they never received; on the free plan that is 3 of 10 daily credits per failure. **(b) history** — the row lists the question under the Research tag exactly like a completed session; since segment 3 it is stored in the `research_v1` shell and renders as an EMPTY expanded body (pre-wrap of `""`) with no references block — indistinguishable in the list from a real answer; older error rows (plain `""`) look the same. Neither /research's error banner nor any marker is persisted.
  - **Class — [HONESTY], reasoned against this file's definition:** both facets are the product telling the user something untrue on live pages — a charge for nothing, and a "session" that never produced an answer. **Rejected [OTHER]:** it would file this as billing hygiene and lose facet (b). **Rejected [COMPLIANCE]:** `pages/refund.tsx` has zero mentions of credits (checked), so no legal-page promise is contradicted — the refund policy is subscription-level. **P2:** a real charge and a false record, but bounded to error events and touching no medical output.
  - **Fix path:** set an `errored` flag in `event_stream()`'s `StreamEventType.ERROR` branch and, in the DONE branch, skip BOTH the history write and the deduction when it is set (or write the row with an explicit error marker and never charge); mirror in the anon branch. Rule 19 before shipping: check whether Explain (its `"done"` after an error event) and Verify share the convention — not audited here. Old error rows: unrecoverable and unidentifiable (an empty `answer` is their only trace).
  - **How to observe locally, no code change:** restart the backend with `$env:GENERATOR_MODEL='gpt-does-not-exist'` (only the Research main path and Explain Stage 3 build on that binding — `generator.py:143`, `api/services/explain_service.py:357`; guards/retrieval/reranker/verify/embedder use their own `*_MODEL` env names), run a literature-hitting Research query, read `user_usage.credits_used_today` for `test_user` before/after, open /history. Recipe + gate rows: recon_20260901 §10 rows 10–11.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-02, segment-3 follow-up; **filed** 2026-09-03.
  - **📏 MEASURED 2026-09-03 (§10 gate row 10) — and the apparent "success charges +1, failure charges +3" inconsistency RESOLVED FROM CODE, not guessed.** Founder readings: two successful Research queries moved `credits_used_today` 5 → 6, one failed query 6 → 9. Derivation: `deduct_credits` (`api/services/usage_service.py:137-148`) adds `CREDIT_COSTS["research"] = 3` unconditionally and is the ONLY deduction on the authed path (`server.py:943`, same call on success and on the ERROR→DONE paths); `reset_daily_if_needed` (`usage_service.py:69-87`) zeroes `credits_used_today` when the UTC date advances, and in TEST_MODE its only live caller is `/api/user/status` (`server.py:2490`), which the Navbar fetches on page load. Dev-DB read (`SELECT … FROM user_usage WHERE clerk_user_id='test_user'`): `last_daily_reset = 2026-09-03 03:21:01Z`, 36 s before the first gate row (id 2334, `03:21:37Z`); the "5" reading was taken on 2026-09-02 UTC. Sequence: 5 → **0 (UTC-midnight reset on page load)** → 3 (2334) → 6 (2335, the founder's "6") → 9 (2336, the error row) → 12 (2337, the zh-TW query) = the DB value read 2026-09-03. **Success and failure both charge exactly 3 — consistent with the pricing table (CLAUDE.md "Research=3", `CREDIT_COSTS`); the only wrong charge is the failure one, which is this entry.** No new class, no new entry. Side facts: `credits_used` (lifetime) stays 0 because `plan_type='pro'` (the counter increments only for free); the reset fires at 00:00 UTC = 08:00 Asia/Taipei, invisible to the user — the meter shows no reset time (note-only, not filed).

- [OTHER] **[P3 · comment drift — surfaced 2026-09-01 by recon_20260901 §7-D5, filed 2026-09-03, NOT fixed] `api/services/deletion_service.py:88` cites its coverage guard as `tests/test_deletion_table_coverage.py`; the file on disk is `tests/test_deletion_coverage.py`**
  - Wrong from birth: `9439699` (2026-08-17) introduced the comment AND added the real file under the other name; no ref ever contained the cited name (`git log --all --diff-filter=A -- tests/test_deletion_table_coverage.py` → empty). The baton checker flags the recon's verbatim quote of the wrong name as "NOT IN REPO" on every run — expected: that quote IS the finding, not a citation to resolve.
  - **Fix:** one-word comment edit (drop `table_`), no test change; take it in any car that touches `deletion_service.py`. Not done here (this car is docs-only).

- [OTHER] **[P3 · markdown rendering — surfaced 2026-09-03 by the segment-3 §10 gate, NOT fixed] `prose` is a NO-OP on /research AND /history: `@tailwindcss/typography` is a dependency but was NEVER registered in `tailwind.config.js` (`plugins: []`), so markdown `- ` lists render as plain lines while the server-rendered share page shows bullets — two Rule-12 consumers, different output for the same markdown**
  - **Derived at HEAD `67db733`:** `package.json:16` lists `@tailwindcss/typography ^0.5.19`; `tailwind.config.js` (Tailwind **3.4**, `tailwindcss ^3.4.19`, config-file plugin model) has `plugins: []` — `git log -S` shows the string `typography` touched only by the initial commit `631b6e5` (2026-02-13) and the `require('@tailwindcss/typography')` registration never existed in any ref. Result: every `prose prose-sm prose-headings:… ` class on `pages/research.tsx:639`/`:653` and `pages/history.tsx:335` generates NO CSS, and Tailwind preflight (`@tailwind base`, `styles/globals.css:1`) leaves `ul/ol { list-style: none; padding: 0 }` in force → no bullets, no list indent. The `--tw-prose-*` token objects both pages set are inert for the same reason. The share page is unaffected because `api/templates/q_base.jinja2:233-238` styles `.vela-prose ul, ol, li::marker` directly.
  - **Provenance — NOT segment 3, NOT segment 1:** the segment-1 half-A renderer copied /research's class + token set verbatim (that was the design: same output as the live page) and inherited the no-op; /research has rendered without bullets since the prose classes were adopted. The gate row 8 "unchanged" verdict is consistent: both pages are identically bullet-less.
  - **Fix path (one line + eye pass):** register the plugin — `plugins: [require('@tailwindcss/typography')]` in `tailwind.config.js` — then eye BOTH pages in both schemes: the prose classes will suddenly take effect everywhere they are written (headings, paragraphs spacing, links, code), so this is a visual change across /research, /history and any other `prose` user (`git grep -n "prose"` to enumerate before flipping). Alternative, narrower: reuse the existing `.markdown-content` rules in `styles/globals.css:52-66` (`ul { list-style-type: disc; padding-left: 2em }`) on the two wrappers — zero blast radius beyond lists.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-03, §10 gate (founder: history bullets flat vs share page bullets present).

- [OTHER] **[P3 · citation abstract cleanup — surfaced 2026-09-03 by the segment-3 §10 gate, pre-existing, NOT fixed] `CitationPanel` shows raw `**MATERIALS AND METHODS:**`, `**ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE:**` and `■` inside abstracts on BOTH /research and /history**
  - **Shared component, so pre-existing:** `components/CitationPanel.tsx` is imported unchanged by `pages/research.tsx:13` and (since segment 3) `pages/history.tsx`; the cleanup is `extractAbstract` (`CitationPanel.tsx:50-72`), whose label strip `\*\*[A-Z][A-Z\s\/]{1,20}:\*\*` (`:69`) matches bold upper-case labels of at most 21 characters — `MATERIALS AND METHODS:` (21 + colon) and `ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE:` (30) exceed it and survive verbatim, and the PubMed structured-abstract glyph `■` is never handled. Abstracts render as plain text (`<p>{display}</p>`), so the `**` markers show literally.
  - **Fix path:** widen the strip to any bold upper-case label (`\*\*[A-Z][A-Z\s\/&,\-]{1,60}:\*\*`) and strip `■`/`▪` glyphs; or render the snippet through the same markdown pipeline. Rule 21 applies — sample real PubMed snippets (positives AND negatives) before widening.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-03, §10 gate.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · i18n / Rule 16 — surfaced 2026-09-03 by the segment-3 §10 gate (zh-TW row), NOT fixed] the /history session-type badge is hard-coded English (`Research` / `Verify` / `Explain`) while the Navbar translates the same words (zh-TW 「研究」) — keys already exist, the page does not use them**
  - **Derived:** `pages/history.tsx:29-37` `FEATURE_LABELS = { research: 'Research', verify: 'Verify', explain: 'Explain' }` feeds `TypeTag`; `utils/i18n-extra.ts` carries `navResearch` / `navVerify` / `navExplain` in all 16 locales (16 quoted values each; zh-TW `navResearch: '研究'`). So the badge is not a proper-noun exemption by design — the product translates the feature name one component up.
  - **Fix path:** map `session_type` → `extra.navResearch` / `navVerify` / `navExplain` (0 new keys); keep the capitalize-fallback for unknown types. Reviewer-round material: none (existing reviewed strings).
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-03, §10 gate row 9 (「參考文獻」 localized beside an English "Research" badge).

- [OTHER] **[P3 · dev hygiene — surfaced 2026-09-03 by the segment-3 run sheet / §10 seeding, NOT fixed] `DATABASE_URL` is read at import in `api/database/sql_db.py:8` with a SILENT SQLite default, and `load_dotenv()` runs only in `api/server.py:9` — any script, probe or one-liner that imports the DB layer without the server (or from a cwd without `.env`) targets `sqlite:///./data/medinotes.db` and reports "no such table" or an empty DB instead of failing loud**
  - **Same root as instrument-blind row #14** (the Phase-1 rewrite-arm harness, 2026-08-06, in the process entry above) — that row recorded the retriever/vector-store variant; this entry records the DB variant surfaced when the gate seed one-liners had to prepend `from dotenv import load_dotenv; load_dotenv()` to reach the Neon dev DB (run sheet 2026-09-03). Rule 18 shape: a fallback that succeeds quietly on the wrong target.
  - **Fix path (small):** in `sql_db.py`, `load_dotenv(find_dotenv())` before the `getenv`, and fail loud (raise) when `DATABASE_URL` is unset unless `TEST_MODE` is set — the SQLite default is a test convenience, not a production posture. Run `python -m pytest -q` after: the test files set `DATABASE_URL` themselves (`sqlite:///:memory:` via `setdefault`) and must keep passing.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-03.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · timestamps shown in the wrong timezone — surfaced 2026-09-03 by the segment-3 §10 gate screenshot, DERIVED from code, NOT fixed] /history shows every record's time as UTC-read-as-local: `created_at` is stored naive-UTC, serialized WITHOUT an offset, and the page parses it as local time — 8 h wrong for every Asia/Taipei user, wrong by the local offset for everyone outside UTC**
  - **Observation:** the §10 gate's first row was written at `03:21:37Z` (dev DB `created_at`, id 2334); the founder's /history screenshot, taken in Asia/Taipei (UTC+8, wall clock ≈ 11:21), read **"Sep 3, 2026, 03:21 AM"**.
  - **Derived at HEAD `8f9306a` (Rule 25 — every step reproduced, not inferred):** **(a) storage** — `api/models/sql_models.py:25` `created_at = Column(DateTime, default=datetime.utcnow)`: a NAIVE UTC datetime in a `timestamp` (not `timestamptz`) column. **(b) wire** — `GET /api/history` `response_model=list[ChatHistoryEntry]` (`api/server.py:1925`) types the field as plain `datetime` (`:1922`, `_history_dt` is just the `datetime` class aliased at `:1905`); serializing a naive value yields **`"2026-09-03T03:21:37.726010"` — no `Z`, no offset** (reproduced: `ChatHistoryEntry(created_at=datetime(2026,9,3,3,21,37,726010)).model_dump_json()` and `jsonable_encoder` both emit exactly that string). **(c) render** — `pages/history.tsx:285` `new Date(item.created_at).toLocaleString('en-US', {…})`: per ECMAScript, a date-TIME string with no offset is parsed as LOCAL time. Reproduced in Node on this machine (tz Asia/Taipei): the naive string formats as **"Sep 3, 2026, 03:21 AM"** (internally `2026-09-02T19:21:37Z`), the same string with `Z` formats as **"Sep 3, 2026, 11:21 AM"**. The screenshot is the naive branch.
  - **Second surface, same defect:** `GET /api/share/list` (`api/server.py:3387`) emits `r.created_at.isoformat()` (`:3408`) on the naive `SharedQuery.created_at` (`sql_models.py:129`, same `utcnow` default); `components/MySharesTab.tsx:43-70` `relativeTime()` / `daysSince()` run `Date.parse(iso)` on it → the "shared N ago" text and the `days_since_created` analytics value are skewed by the viewer's UTC offset (a share made a minute ago reads as ~8 h old in Taipei; `daysSince` rounds a day early). **Not affected:** /verify and /explain show no timestamps; the public share pages (`q_public.jinja2` / `q_base.jinja2`) print no `created_at`; `_cleanup_old_records` compares naive-to-naive on the server and is correct. The other six `created_at` columns with the same default (`sql_models.py:64/78/95/111/151/179`) are not rendered to users today — same trap if they ever are.
  - **Class — [HONESTY], reasoned:** the row is the user's own record and the page states a time for it that is factually wrong by hours; that is the product telling the user something untrue, on a live page, for 100 % of rows and every non-UTC user. **Rejected [OTHER]:** this is not formatting polish — the displayed value is a different instant. **P2:** breadth (every row, both surfaces) against low fix cost and no medical content involved.
  - **Fix path (server-side, both endpoints — Rule 19: fix the field once at the response layer, not per page):** emit an aware value — in `ChatHistoryEntry` a `@field_serializer('created_at')` returning `v.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc).isoformat()` (→ `…+00:00`), and the same `replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)` before `.isoformat()` at `:3408`; the page code then needs NO change (`new Date` honours the offset, `toLocaleString` renders the viewer's local time). Longer-term: `DateTime(timezone=True)` + `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` defaults — a `timestamp → timestamptz` migration on Postgres, NOT this car. **Do not** patch client-side by appending `'Z'` — it silently double-shifts the moment the server starts emitting an offset. Test: an endpoint-level assertion that the serialized `created_at` ends with `+00:00`/`Z`, in the `tests/test_history_delete.py` harness.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-03 (§10 gate screenshot); **derived + filed** 2026-09-03 at the car close.
  - **STATUS 2026-09-03 — BUILT, LOCAL, NOT pushed — prod still fly 244.** HISTORY HONESTY car, segment 1; code commit `88ddde5` (`_utc_isoformat` + `@field_serializer("created_at")` on `ChatHistoryEntry`; the same helper at the `/api/share/list` site; no frontend change, no column change). AFTER wire: `"2026-09-03T03:21:37+00:00"`. Tests +4 in `tests/test_history_delete.py` (suite 414 → 418 passed / 28 skipped); Rule 20 evidence `tests/probes/history_timezone/repro.mjs` → `result.json` (03:21 AM vs 11:21 AM under Asia/Taipei). Three date-only third surfaces found and NOT fixed (founder's call) — `docs/batons/history_honesty_car_20260903.md` §2a. **Gate §1 BLANK, founder fills**; entry stays OPEN until it ships. **Fixup `ed181ed` (same day):** the serializer returns an AWARE datetime instead of a `str`, so `/openapi.json` keeps `type: string, format: date-time` (the `str` return had downgraded it to a bare string); consequence, stated: the `/api/history` wire is now `"2026-09-03T03:21:37Z"` (Pydantic 2.8 writes UTC as `Z`), `/api/share/list` still `+00:00` — same instant, both RFC 3339, both parsed identically (probe re-run with `Z` cases). +1 test pinning the schema fragment (suite 419 / 28).
- [OTHER] **[P3 · naive datetimes on three DATE-ONLY exits — surfaced 2026-09-03 by the HISTORY HONESTY car segment-1 pre-flight (Rule 25 enumeration), NOT fixed; founder ruled 2026-09-03: NOT this car, note only] three more naive-UTC datetimes leave the API / templates without an offset — none renders a wall-clock TIME to a user today, so this is the timezone entry's defect class at a lower blast radius**
  - **The three exits (unit: endpoints / render sites, derived — `git grep -nE '"[a-z_]+_at"\s*:' -- api/server.py` · `git grep -n "isoformat" -- api/` · `git grep -n "published_at\|updated_at\|created_at" -- '*.jinja2'`):** **(1)** `GET /api/user/context/hash` (`api/server.py`, `user_context_hash_get`) emits `row.updated_at.isoformat()` on the naive `UserProfile.updated_at` (`sql_models.py:152`) — the only consumer, `utils/contextSync.ts:84-91`, reads `user_context_hash` + `locale` and never `updated_at`: NOT rendered. **(2)** Blog: `BlogPost.published_at` / `updated_at` (`sql_models.py:178/180`, naive; set by `datetime.utcnow()` or front-matter in `scripts/blog_cli.py`) → `api/services/blog_renderer.py:262-264` and `:344` `.isoformat()` → `api/templates/blog_post.jinja2:14-15` JSON-LD `datePublished` / `dateModified` as a NAIVE ISO string (crawlers), and `:54` `<time datetime="{{ published_at_iso }}">{{ published_at_iso[:10] }}</time>` (humans, DATE only); `blog_list.jinja2:22` and `:58-59` the same. **(3)** Explore: `ExplorePage.last_updated_at` (`sql_models.py:204`) → `api/services/explore_renderer.py:372` `strftime("%Y-%m-%d")` → `api/templates/category_listing.jinja2:20` (DATE only).
  - **Why P3, not the timezone entry's P2:** a date-only render is wrong only when the UTC date differs from the viewer's local date — within 8 h of midnight UTC for Asia/Taipei — and schema.org `datePublished` is valid as an ISO 8601 date or date-time without an offset; nothing here shows a TIME. Exit (1) is not rendered at all.
  - **Fix path (when a car takes it):** the segment-1 `_utc_isoformat` helper at the two `.isoformat()` sites (`user_context_hash_get`; `blog_renderer.py:262-264` + `:344`) — one line each, Rule 19 carry-across; the two `strftime` DATE sites are correct as UTC dates and need only a decision on whether "published on" means the UTC date or the author's local date. Long-term the `timestamp → timestamptz` migration named in the timezone entry covers all three.
  - **Cross-ref:** the `[HONESTY][P2] timestamps shown in the wrong timezone` entry directly above (same defect class, the two user-facing TIME surfaces, fixed in segment 1) · `docs/batons/history_honesty_car_20260903.md` §2a (the Rule 25 claimed-vs-derived table these three came from).
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-03 (segment-1 pre-flight); **filed** 2026-09-03 (segment-1 fixup docs commit).

- [OTHER] **[P3 · Rule 4 print() + cp950 console crash — surfaced 2026-09-02 by the Segment-1 gate prep, NOT fixed] `TFDACorpusStore._load_compact` fallback `print()` with emoji CRASHES the server at import under a cp950 console**
  - **What happened:** starting the documented dev flow (`uvicorn api.server:app`) in a Windows cp950 terminal crashed at import — the TFDA indication-corpus load raised, and the fail-soft handler itself died: `UnicodeEncodeError: 'cp950' codec can't encode character '⚠'` at `api/database/vector_store.py:182`, the line `print(f"⚠️ TFDA indication corpus load failed ({e}) — TFDA source disabled")` inside `TFDACorpusStore._load_compact`. A fail-soft path that crashes is fail-loud in the wrong direction: it also **masks the underlying load error** (`{e}` never printed).
  - **Class + siblings:** same cp950 console class as `check_baton.py:438`; `_load_compact` has THREE emoji `print()` calls (`:177` row-mismatch, `:180` success, `:182` load-failure) — all are **Rule 4 violations** (`print()` instead of `logging.getLogger()`), and migrating them to logging fixes both the rule and the crash in one move.
  - **Workaround (why P3, stated):** `PYTHONUTF8=1` before launch avoids it entirely; prod runs in a UTF-8 Linux container and is untouched — dev-only, easy workaround, hence P3 not P2.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-09-02, HISTORY-car Segment-1 gate prep (recorded in the gate-prep report).

- [OTHER] **[P2 · judge-prompt ↔ spec drift — surfaced by the 2026-08-25 defect-2 recon (Ruling 2(a)'s mapping check), NOT fixed] 🔴 PRD §2.7 需求 3 ↔ `explain_judge` drift: the dimension named for citation-source validity FAILS the sources the spec REQUIRES, and the check the spec actually states is UNIMPLEMENTED**
  - **(i)** `citation_source_types_valid` (`api/prompts/explain_judge.md:68-77`) whitelists `{LOINC, RxNorm, MedlinePlus, FDA}` and **FAILS PubMed/NICE/Cochrane — the sources 需求 3 (`docs/PRD.md:621-627`) REQUIRES for clinical judgment** (*"臨床判斷類(必須用 PubMed、FDA、NICE、Cochrane、國家指引)"*). The judge's own documented ground is Path-1 pragmatics (*"Explain has no PubMed retrieval; PubMed citations would be fabricated"* — `explain_judge.md:72`), so the divergence is deliberate-at-the-time, not accidental — but it is drift against the spec's letter all the same.
  - **(ii)** The requirement's actual check — **LOINC-not-primary for clinical judgment** (需求 3, and 驗收標準 `docs/PRD.md:672` *"所有涉及臨床判斷的項目,citation 不只有 LOINC"*) — is **UNIMPLEMENTED: LOINC passes unconditionally in every dimension.**
  - **Fix path and why it is DEFERRED:** any fix edits `api/prompts/explain_judge.md`, which is a judge-prompt change → **a §2.7 ExplainJudge re-baseline (CLAUDE.md Rule 17)**. Batch with the next judge re-baseline — same disposition as the existing explain_system.md FDA-instruction entry.
  - **Classed [OTHER]:** it misreports to a developer running the judge, not to a user.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-25, by the defect-2 recon executing Ruling 2(a)'s mapping condition. The SUPPLEMENTAL RULING 2026-08-25 (recorded in the defect-2 entry) rests partly on this finding: it demoted this dimension to report-only and re-grounded `no_fabricated_citations` on founder-ruling authority instead of PRD §2.7.

- [DONE] **[P2 · instrument integrity / silent mis-resolution — surfaced 2026-08-23, RULE + FIX shipped 2026-08-24] ✅ THE source_id DIGIT-SCRAPE CLASS IS CLOSED BY A RULE, NOT BY A LIST**

- [OTHER] **[P2 · process / how a false finding was produced — filed 2026-08-24, NOT a code defect] 🔴 A FRESH MEASUREMENT CONTRADICTED THIS FILE, AND THE RECON OVERRODE THE LEDGER INSTEAD OF ESTABLISHING WHY. THE LEDGER WAS RIGHT.**
  - **What happened.** A read-only recon reported that `api/services/direction_checker.py:86` and `api/services/retrieval_refusal.py:76` run the digit scrape **unguarded**, and sized it as a possible product defect. **Both are guarded**, at `:83-84` and `:73-74`. The recon's grep matched the regex line and looked for `SourceType.PUBMED` on the **same line** — the spelling used at `scripts/question_neutralization_eval.py:59-60` — so the **preceding-line `if ... != SourceType.PUBMED: continue`** form was invisible to it.
  - **🔑 THE PART THAT MAKES THIS A PROCESS ENTRY, NOT A TYPO.** This file **already recorded the guards correctly, twice** — the *"`RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` CANNOT SEE THE SMALL-POOL CASES"* bullet and the `api/server.py:571-572` early-return bullet, **both** citing `retrieval_refusal.py:73-74` as *"skips every non-PubMed `source_type`"*. The recon read neither. It produced a measurement, saw it disagree with the ledger, and **wrote the ledger off** rather than asking which was wrong.
  - **THE LESSON, stated so it is applicable: when a fresh measurement and the ledger disagree, ESTABLISH WHY BEFORE WRITING EITHER ONE OFF.** This repo's habit — correctly, and for reasons recorded across many entries — is *measurement beats memory*. **This is the case where the ledger was right and the instrument was blind**, and that habit is exactly what made the error easy to commit. A disagreement is a third fact, not a tiebreak.
  - **CHECK, in the form a future reader can apply:** before reporting that something the ledger records is absent, `git grep` the ledger for the file being measured and read what it says. If it disagrees, the report states **both** readings and reconciles them — it does not silently pick one.
  - **Why `[OTHER]` and not `[HONESTY]`:** this file defines `[HONESTY]` as *the product tells the USER something untrue*. This misled a developer reading a recon, not a user. Filing it under `[HONESTY]` would inflate the one listing this repo uses to enumerate user-facing honesty debt.
  - ⚠️ **Both recon files are preserved verbatim and NOT corrected** — `docs/batons/recon_20260824_step8_and_pmid.md` (the false Tier-3 finding) and `docs/batons/recon_20260824_tier3_reachability.md` (its supersession, itself carrying a miscount). Each has a dated addendum header above an unaltered body. **The error is the artifact; editing it away would destroy the only worked example.**
  - **Surfaced + filed:** 2026-08-24, by the reachability recon that disproved the first one. **Open** — a process entry closes by not recurring, not by a commit.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · Rule 7 CHECK baseline — filed at rule adoption 2026-08-18, NOT fixed] 5 bare-INSERT direct-commit sites owe migration to `_safe_db_write()` or a reason comment**
  - **現況**: CLAUDE.md Rule 7 was rewritten 2026-08-18 (founder-ratified) to cover the whole `api/` tree, with a CHECK: *every `db.commit()` outside the helper either is DML the helper cannot express, or carries a call-site comment naming its (a)/(b) reason.* **At adoption the CHECK fails at exactly these 5 sites** (re-derived at HEAD, symbols + current lines): `explain_identify_feedback` (`api/server.py:1630`) · `create_feedback` (`:1732`) · **`dodo_webhook` — the IGNORED-EVENT branch only** (`:2091`, bare `db.add(WebhookEvent)` + commit; **the MAIN dodo path at `:2174` is class (a)** — a multi-statement UPDATE+INSERT transaction — and is NOT in scope here) · `cost_tracker.log_api_cost` (`api/services/cost_tracker.py:55`) · `cost_tracker.log_api_cost_standalone` (`:85`). **This entry is the debt ledger; the rule text carries only the baseline count — neither doc claims the other's job.**
  - 🔴 **`explain_identify_feedback` and `create_feedback` carry a REAL CORRECTNESS DEFECT, not just nonconformance:** their hand-rolled `except Exception` blocks (`:1632-1634`, `:1734-1736`) **never call `db.rollback()`** — a failed INSERT leaves the session dirty for whatever touches it next. They re-implement the helper's contract minus its rollback. **Migrating them to `_safe_db_write()` fixes conformance and the defect in one move.**
  - **`log_api_cost` / `log_api_cost_standalone`: migration is SAFE, and Rule 13 is an argument FOR it, not against.** Rule 13's never-block intent is **compatible** with the helper's swallow-and-return-False semantics — the Rule 13 `try/except` wrappers sit at the callers. Also an internal inconsistency the migration would normalize: **`log_api_cost_standalone` does `rollback + raise` (`:86-88`, inside its own outer catch at `:91`) while `log_api_cost` has no visible catch at the site** — two cost-logging paths, two different failure behaviours today.
  - **Fix cost: small, mechanical, five call-site edits.** None is a medical-output path — feedback capture, webhook bookkeeping, cost logging. **Per-site gates beyond pytest: NONE owed** — no prompt changes (no §2.7 re-baseline), no render changes (no human-eye gate), no schema changes (no migration). Standard suite + tsc suffices; the dodo branch edit warrants one extra look because it sits in a payment webhook file, but the branch itself only records ignored events.
  - **Surfaced**: 2026-08-18, by the Rule 7 adoption census (25 commit sites → 24 non-helper → 16 class (a) + 3 class (b) + these 5).

- [COMPLIANCE] **[P2 · legal-page promise vs shipped capability — surfaced 2026-08-18, NOT fixed] 🔴 `/privacy` §4 tells users they can delete their CHAT HISTORY. There is no way to delete anything — `@app.delete` appears ZERO times in `api/`**
  - **Verbatim at HEAD, `pages/privacy.tsx:50`** (executable JSX, rendered text — not a comment), under the §4 heading `4. Data Retention, Deletion, and User Rights` (`:45`): *"When you delete your account **or chat history**, the eligible personal data (excluding the statutory financial records described above) will be immediately and permanently deleted from our active production databases."*
  - **CLASSIFIED `[COMPLIANCE]`, NOT `[HONESTY]`, AND THE REASON IS THE PHRASING.** The sentence is **conditional** — *"when you delete…"* — so it never states that the affordance exists and is not, strictly, a false statement; that is what keeps it out of `[HONESTY]`, whose test in this file is *"the product currently tells the user something untrue."* What puts it in `[COMPLIANCE]` is **where** it sits: a data-subject-rights section of a live legal page, describing an erasure right, where a reasonable reader takes "when you delete your chat history" as a statement that they can. **It is a representation about a right, on the document that defines the contract.**
  - **THE CAPABILITY IS ABSENT, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED:** `@app.delete` in `api/` = **0**. `router.delete` = **0**. `methods=[… "DELETE"]` = **0**. `APIRouter`/`include_router` = **0**, so no sub-router can hide one. Frontend `method: 'DELETE'` across all `*.ts*` = **0**. The `api/` tree has **38 route decorators: 20 `@app.get`, 18 `@app.post`, and nothing else.** `@app.get("/api/history")` (`api/server.py:1812`) is the only history endpoint, and `pages/history.tsx` contains no match for `delete|clear|remove|刪除`.
  - ⚠️ **A SECOND UNBACKED CLAIM IS IN THE SAME PARAGRAPH and is easy to miss because the quote usually stops early:** the sentence continues — *"Residual copies … within our automated system backups and transaction histories will naturally expire and be **completely overwritten within 24 hours**."* Nothing in the repo implements or evidences a 24-hour backup-overwrite window.
  - ⚠️ **A THIRD promise, `pages/privacy.tsx:82` (§8):** *"If you delete your account, the link between you and any pages you previously shared is removed…"* — the sever exists in `deletion_service.py` but is **unreachable from any route**, so it is manual-only like the rest.
  - 🔴 **THE SCENARIO THAT MAKES THIS LIVE RATHER THAN THEORETICAL:** Clerk's `<UserButton>` **is mounted** (`components/Navbar.tsx:343`), and it lets a user delete their Clerk identity from inside the product. **That severs their login while leaving every Vela row intact.** `/privacy:48` papers over exactly this with a `mailto:` asking such users to contact support. So the one deletion affordance a user actually has in the UI is the one that deletes the least.
  - **EXPOSURE IS ONE ENGLISH PAGE, NOT SIXTEEN — checked, because the sibling retention entry found the opposite shape.** `pages/privacy.tsx` imports only `next/link` and `next/image`; there is **no `useTranslation`, no `t(`, no i18n import**, no `[locale]` privacy route, and `next.config.ts` has `output: 'export'` with **no `i18n`/`locales` key at all**. The clause exists in **no** i18n file. ⚠️ **But the LINK to it is translated into all 16 locales** (`utils/i18n-ui.ts` `privacyPolicy` ×16, `utils/i18n.ts` `privacyPolicyLink` ×16) — **users in 16 locales are routed to an English promise.** *(Contrast the retention entry below: "6 months" is English-only on `/privacy` while the FAQ's "180 days" IS keyed across 16 locales. Different shape, same family.)*
  - **NOT FIXED, and the fix is not a wording tweak alone** — either the capability ships (see BACKLOG "Deletion-feature C", where the per-entry scope is now recorded) or the clause is narrowed. **Which one is a founder decision with counsel in the loop; this entry does not choose.**
  - ✍️ **2026-09-02 (delete segment — dated annotation only, entry stays OPEN):** per-entry chat-history delete **now exists in code** — owner-checked `DELETE /api/history/{id}` + frontend control, local commits `9a3dd15`/`74df707`, **pending deploy** (prod still has zero delete routes until it ships). This partially discharges the §4 chat-history clause once deployed; the account-deletion half, the 24-hour backup-overwrite claim (second bullet above) and the §8 share-severing claim remain unbacked. **Entry closure is the founder's call, not this car's.**
  - **Surfaced**: 2026-08-18, from a read-only scan that had never been written down.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · public API surface — surfaced 2026-08-18, NOT fixed] `/openapi.json` publishes internal engineering prose on FIVE more surfaces after `d3c0d95` fixed the Clerk handler — and the WORST one is a component schema, not an endpoint**
  - **Method: verified against the GENERATED schema, not against any prior report.** 37 operations, **9** publish a description.
  - 🔴 **THE BIGGEST LEAK IS `CitationIn.description` — a 1,483-character internal engineering memo, longer than all 9 operation descriptions COMBINED** (source: the docstring at `api/server.py:2981-2982`). It names source files (`api/models/schemas.py:Citation`, `ShareModal.tsx`, `share_renderer._augment_citations`), a **P2 debt item**, the internal cohort ids **`open #7`** and **`pre-c1`**, and states on a public surface exactly which validation posture was chosen and why unknown values are deliberately accepted. **An endpoint-only audit misses this entirely — the `d3c0d95` sweep looked at operations and this is a component.**
  - **`POST /api/explain` publishes exact model names — `GPT-4.1-mini` and `GPT-4.1`.** Checked the whole schema blob for `GPT|gpt-|claude|groq|openai|anthropic|llama|o1|o3`: **these are the ONLY model/vendor strings in it; all seven other terms return zero.** Two problems at once: **commercially sensitive while a provider evaluation is live**, and **it goes stale the moment a model changes** — the same class of public stale assertion `d3c0d95` just fixed on the Clerk handler.
  - **Internal doc structure: THREE operation descriptions, not four.** `PRD § 4.5` (`track-citation-click`), `PRD § 4.6 PHASE D` (`explore/category`), `§4.3 需求 5` (`GET /api/user/context/hash`). ⚠️ **The `P3 TECH_DEBT` mention is NOT a fourth description — it is the closing sentence of the PRD § 4.5 one.** Plus two more the endpoint audit missed: `POST /api/user/context/hash` names the internal requirement id **`E3`**, and `UserContextHashRequest.description` names **`PRD §3.1 v1.6`** — so the true `PRD` count in the schema is **3**, spread across operations *and* components.
  - **The two tracking endpoints — and a correction to how they were described.** They are `POST /api/share/track-visit` and `POST /api/share/track-citation-click`. **Only ONE of the two says "No-op handler" and "No DB write"** (`track-citation-click`); `track-visit` says *"we just log the visit and return 204"*, which implies it without saying it. Do not write "two endpoints advertise no DB write." Both docstrings are **accurate** to their bodies (a guard, one `logger.info`, `return Response(status_code=204)`).
  - 🔴 **AND THEY ARE LIVE, WHICH INVERTS THE WORRY.** The Next.js app **never calls them** — grep of `pages/ components/ utils/ contexts/` finds no caller (the single `track('citation_clicked')` hit in `components/CitationPanel.tsx:90` is a PostHog call, not HTTP). **The callers are in the server-rendered Jinja2 share page** — `api/templates/q_public.jinja2:123` (`sendBeacon`, with `fetch` fallbacks at `:128`/`:132`) and `:152`/`:154` — rendered by `share_renderer.py:496` for every `GET /q/{share_id}`. **So they receive traffic on every public share view and every citation click, and the public schema tells any reader that this telemetry is discarded.**
  - **Also published, not previously listed:** `ResearchRequest.response_language.description` names the private helper **`_resolve_response_language`**; the `SourceType` enum publishes the retrieval corpus inventory `["pubmed","fda","local","tfda","dailymed"]` — including the deprecated internal tier name `local`, whose continued acceptance `CitationIn`'s memo then explains.
  - ⚠️ **`components.securitySchemes` is `null` and 0 of 37 operations declare `security`** — so the schema advertises `GET /api/admin/costs` (real handler `api/server.py:2507-2515`, gated at runtime by `require_auth` + `ADMIN_USER_ID`) as though it were unauthenticated. **Runtime is correctly gated; the published contract does not say so.**
  - ✅ **Clean, and worth recording so it is not re-checked:** no `servers` block; **zero** occurrences of `http://`, `https://`, `localhost`, `fly.dev` or `sk-`; response descriptions are only `"Successful Response"` ×37 and `"Validation Error"` ×20 — **no `str(e)` leakage, Rule 5 holds.**
  - ⚠️ **Rule 16 adjacent, flagged not fixed:** the public API contract is not language-consistent — `ResearchRequest`, `VerifyRequest`, `SourceType` and 6 field descriptions are **zh-TW only**, while `ExplainRequest.report_text` is English only.
  - **The discipline already exists and was applied once:** `api/server.py:2181-2189` states the hazard in `#` comments and the Clerk description is a clean 385-char contract. **It was not applied to `CitationIn`, `track-visit`, `track-citation-click`, `explore/category`, or `UserContextHashRequest`.**
  - **Surfaced**: 2026-08-18. **Reported, not rewritten** — none of these was judged as clearly inappropriate as the Clerk case, and rewriting public contract text is a founder call.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · Rule 20 / evidence reproducibility — surfaced 2026-08-17, confirmed 2026-08-18, NOT fixed] 🔴 The `291 passed / 28 skipped` pytest baseline cited as evidence in several STATE entries has NO REPRODUCIBLE PATH — plain `pytest` aborts**
  - **The number is real but the command is not written down anywhere.** It reproduced **only** as `python -m pytest -q --ignore=tests/test_webhook_cancel.py --ignore=tests/results` (verified — exact match). ⚠️ **FACTUAL CORRECTION 2026-08-21: the `--ignore=tests/test_webhook_cancel.py` half is now OBSOLETE** — `ca4bb7d` (2026-08-19) renamed that file to `scripts/smoke_webhook_cancel.py` and made it skip-safe, so **plain `python -m pytest -q` now collects and runs** (re-verified 2026-08-21: **316 passed / 28 skipped**). The `tests/results` half of the problem is unaddressed and the entry stays OPEN. **There is no `pytest.ini`, no `[tool.pytest]` in `pyproject.toml`, no `conftest.py` `collect_ignore`.** Those exclusions live **only in shell history**. **This is a Rule 20 failure in the same shape as the reference incident: a number in a doc that no one else can regenerate.**
  - **What a newcomer actually sees:** `INTERNALERROR> SystemExit: 1`, and **zero tests run**. Cause: **`tests/test_webhook_cancel.py` was a MANUAL script, not a test** — it called `sys.exit(1)` at **module scope** when `TEST_USER_EMAIL`/`TEST_USER_ID` were unset, which aborted collection for the entire suite. Its own docstring documented it as a `uv run python tests/…` invocation. ⚠️ **FACTUAL CORRECTION 2026-08-21 — past tense is now correct: this specific cause was REMOVED by `ca4bb7d` (2026-08-19)**; the file lives at `scripts/smoke_webhook_cancel.py` and its module-scope gates are `pytest.skip(..., allow_module_level=True)`. *(Its absence from the suite is now its own debt — see the payment-surface coverage entry.)*
  - **Two more collection faults behind it:** (i) bare `uv run pytest` fails with **5** errors, not 1, because `tests/` has **no `__init__.py`** while `tests/models|providers|services` **do** — so `api` is importable only via `python -m`'s implicit cwd on `sys.path`; (ii) pytest collects **`tests/results/test_output.txt`** and dies on a `UnicodeDecodeError` — and **`tests/results/` is the very gitignored scratch directory named in CLAUDE.md's Rule 20 reference incident.**
  - **The same script also uses `print()` at module scope** (Rule 4) — as a manual script rather than product code, so noted, not escalated. ⚠️ **Location corrected 2026-08-21: it is `scripts/smoke_webhook_cancel.py`, not `tests/test_webhook_cancel.py`** (`git mv` in `ca4bb7d`, recorded in the ruling bullet below).
  - ~~**📋 NOT FIXED — pinning a collection contract is a founder decision.**~~ ✅ **FOUNDER RULED 2026-08-19: (b) + (c) APPLIED, (a) EXPLICITLY DECLINED — belt and braces: (c) fixes the CLASS (any future manual script copied from this one inherits the safe pattern), (b) fixes the instance and the naming.** The original three candidates are retained below as the decision record.
    - **(c) applied:** BOTH module-scope `sys.exit(1)` gates (the TEST_USER_EMAIL/TEST_USER_ID one **and** the DODO_WEBHOOK_SECRET one two lines below it — converting only the named first would have left a mixed pattern, defeating (c)'s stated fix-the-class purpose) are now `pytest.skip(..., allow_module_level=True)`. The three **function-level** `sys.exit(1)`s remain — they fire at runtime, not collection, and are correct for a manual script.
    - **(b) applied:** `git mv tests/test_webhook_cancel.py scripts/smoke_webhook_cancel.py` (history survives). Name derived from the existing convention — `scripts/` holds six `smoke_*` manual scripts that exercise a running backend (this script's exact shape) and zero `manual_*` precedents.
    - ~~🔑 **THE BASELINE COMMAND GOING FORWARD: `python -m pytest -q --ignore=tests/results`** — one ignore flag retired. **`--ignore=tests/results` is STILL REQUIRED, because (a) was declined**: the UTF-16 `tests/results/test_output.txt` remains on the collection path.~~ ✅ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-24 — THE SECOND FLAG IS RETIRED TOO. THE BASELINE COMMAND IS NOW BARE `python -m pytest -q`.** Measured at `efb39d4`: **342 passed / 28 skipped, exit 0, collection clean**, and re-measured at `c9078ce`: **352 / 28** (+10 = this car's new test file). **⚠️ WHY, established rather than assumed — and the reason is not a fix.** The blocker was `tests/results/test_output.txt`, the one file in that directory matching pytest's default `test_*` pattern. **It no longer exists on disk.** Every remaining file in `tests/results/` is prefixed `_` (`_c2_ab_retrieval.py`, `_severity_probe.py`, …), which matches **neither** `test_*.py` **nor** `*_test.py`, so nothing there is collected. **(a) was never applied — there is still NO `pytest.ini`, `setup.cfg`, `pyproject.toml`, `tox.ini` or root `conftest.py` anywhere in the repo** (checked, all five absent).
    - **Empirical results at the ruling commit:** the new command collects cleanly and reproduces **291 passed / 28 skipped**; the OLD full command (now `--ignore`-ing a nonexistent path) also still gives **291/28** — pytest does not error on ignoring a missing path, so stale muscle memory degrades gracefully rather than breaking.
  - ~~🟡 **OPEN RESIDUAL — what keeps this entry open:** `tests/results/` is still collectible by bare `pytest`. Closable only by **(a)** (a `pytest.ini`/`norecursedirs`, which the founder declined for now) **or by a Rule-20-compatible relocation of the scratch directory**. Until one of those happens, anyone running plain `pytest` without the one remaining flag still hits the `UnicodeDecodeError`.~~ ✅ **THE SYMPTOM IS GONE 2026-08-24; THE ENTRY STAYS OPEN, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE POINT.**
    - **What closed: the CLAIM.** Bare `python -m pytest -q` collects cleanly and needs no flag. Any STATE or TECH_DEBT line still saying `--ignore=tests/results` is required is now false.
    - 🔴 **What did NOT close: the CLASS.** Neither ruled-out option happened. `tests/results/` is **still on the collection path**, still gitignored (`.gitignore:113`), and there is still **no collection contract pinned anywhere**. The blocker file was deleted — and because it was **untracked scratch, its deletion left no commit, no date and no author**. The suite went green by an event the repo cannot see. **Drop any `test_*.txt` or `test_*.py` into `tests/results/` tomorrow and the abort returns**, with nothing to warn you.
    - **Why this is NOT re-tagged `[DONE]`:** this file's own 2026-08-18 lesson is that a `[DONE]` tag on a half-open entry feeds the nav counts a wrong number and hides the open half. The unpinned-collection-contract half is open. ⚠️ **Founder call, flagged not taken:** option (a) — a `pytest.ini` with `norecursedirs = tests/results` — would convert "green because a file happens to be absent" into "green because a rule says so". It was declined in 2026-08-19 on different facts (a file that then existed); the facts have changed.
    - ⚠️ **Unrelated drift noticed while establishing the above, reported not fixed:** `tests/results/` is gitignored, yet **six `.py` files in it are TRACKED** (`_citation_url_audit.py`, `_discriminator_validation.py`, `_mem_attribution.py`, `_otc_singledrug_probe.py`, `_poolsize_harvest.py`, `_severity_probe.py`). That contradicts CLAUDE.md Rule 20's split — `tests/probes/` is committed evidence, `tests/results/` is scratch. Whether those six belong in `tests/probes/` is a founder call.
  - **Surfaced**: 2026-08-17 while verifying an unrelated change; the invocation was reverse-engineered rather than found written down, which is the finding.

R06 appears in 33 of the 110 local `golden_results_*.json`. **NO committed artifact records an R06 PASS/FAIL**: the only tracked mention is `docs/poolsize_distribution_harvest.md:42` (`| R06 | [5, 5, 5] | 5 | 5 | 5 | 0.0 | 1.0 |`), which records *pool stability* and states at `:24-26` that verdicts that round were **"NOT MEASURED, not estimated"**. R06 is never individually named in any golden ledger in `STATE.md` / `TECH_DEBT.md` / `BACKLOG.md` / `docs/` — those name only R01, R03, R08, R10, R15, R16, R20.

  - ✅ **The canary half IS fixed, this baton:** `tests/probes/canary/` now carries the gate script **and** its result JSON, per Rule 20. Before it, the five canary IDs appeared in exactly two tracked lines in the whole repo (`docs/pair_aware_retrieval_probe.md:206-207`) and only as *results* — the query strings themselves lived only in gitignored scripts.
  - **➕ APPENDED 2026-08-24 (source_id guard car) — THIS EXPOSURE NOW HAS A SECOND REASON, AND ONE OF THE AFFECTED CLAIMS IS LIVE.** Until today the problem was that the artifact is *gone*. It is now also that **a correct re-run legitimately produces DIFFERENT numbers**, because `c9078ce` fixed the scraper those runs used. Both reasons apply at once and neither substitutes for the other.
    - 🔴 **LEAD ITEM, because it is a claim this repo currently makes: `STATE.md:75` — *"Stage 2 — citation-truth, 18 usable answers (`citation_truth_20260614_113716.json`): PMID existence **80/80 (100%, no fabricated PMIDs)**"*.** That `exist_ok`/`exist_total` tally **is** the metric this car changed, and the 80/80 was produced by the **PRE-GUARD** scraper, which scraped digits out of non-PubMed source_ids too. 🔑 **A bogus scrape that RESOLVES counts as `exist_ok`** — the measured DailyMed scrape `84432` is a real PubMed id — so a non-PubMed citation could inflate **both** halves of that fraction and read as a clean 100%. **The "no fabricated PMIDs" reading may therefore be partly an artifact of the defect.**
    - ⚠️ **UNCHECKABLE, and that is the whole point of this entry:** `citation_truth_20260614_113716.json` is **untracked** (`STATE.md:848` lists it among the gitignored artifacts — *2026-08-27: that entry's full text moved verbatim to `docs/archive/state_shipped_2026.md` under the C1-30d archive rule; its STATE line is the one-line tombstone, heading retained. The `:848` pin is as-of `e0b545f` and drifts with ordinary STATE edits — grep the stable heading anchor `Pair-aware retrieval READ-ONLY scope probe` instead; the artifact list lives in the archive copy*), so whether any of those 80 came from a non-PubMed citation **cannot be determined**. Not restated as a finding, not corrected in place — recorded here as a claim whose basis can no longer be inspected.

And **`pubmed_pool` (`retrieval_recall_check.py:72`) and `pool` (`pathb_recall_probe.py:82`) were ALREADY guarded and do not move**, so pool-level numbers stay comparable across the change while final-stage numbers do not. Cited at `BACKLOG.md:793-794, 796` · `STATE.md:84, 86, 171, 185` · `TECH_DEBT.md:1403, 1410` · `retrieval_refusal_planback.md:82`.

The damage is confined to committed **prose** citing untracked runs, which is exactly what this entry already describes.

- [HONESTY] **[P1 · citation SUPPORT — a NEW failure mode, distinct from wrong-drug, and INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE; found 2026-08-23 by the founder-directed human review, N=1, NOT fixed] 🔴 R12: every citation is real, relevant and correctly attributed — and none of them carries the inference the answer draws. Call it WRONG-SUPPORT**
  - **The instance.** `R12` — *"is it ok to give both blood thinners at the same time"*. The answer is confident and well-structured. The specific clinical scenarios it discusses (**DOAC + antiplatelet**, **warfarin + aspirin**) are supported by a PubMed citation (**PMID 30691551**). Its **three DailyMed citations — `CLOPIDOGREL BISULFATE`, `TIROFIBAN HYDROCHLORIDE MONOHYDRATE`, `UROKINASE` — are three DIFFERENT mechanism classes, each documenting its OWN bleeding risk. Not one is a drug PAIR.**
  - **🔴 WHY THIS IS A NEW AXIS, NOT A VARIANT OF WRONG-DRUG.** Wrong-drug asks *"whose label is this?"* and the answer here is **correct every time** — each cited label genuinely belongs to an anticoagulant/antiplatelet, and each is genuinely about bleeding. **The defect is that the cited documents do not carry the claim.** A citation can be real, on-topic, correctly attributed **and still not be evidence for the sentence it is attached to.**
  - **🔴 NO GATE MEASURES THIS — verified against each gate's actual criterion, not assumed:**
    - **canary** — `evaluate()` fails on `q["wrong_object_cited"] > 0` (`canary_gate.py:338`): drug **identity** + section class. Not support.
    - **danger-path** — v1 `tfda_cited > 0` (source **type**); v3 `safety_is_wrong AND implies_clearance` (**clearance**). Not support.
    - **human-eye ownership form** — scores `EXPECTED OWNER` vs `OBSERVED OWNER(S)`: **identity**. Not support.
    - **§2.7 golden** — `HARD_FLOOR_DIMENSIONS = ["citation_source_types_valid", "no_fabricated_citations"]`: source type and fabrication, and **Explain-path** at that. Not support.
  - **⚠️ TWO PARTIAL EXCEPTIONS, named because the claim "no gate checks this" is otherwise slightly too strong:**
    - **The danger-path's v2 `leans_on_descriptive` IS a support-shaped judgement** — its judge asks *"does the answer answer the SAFETY question by citing what the drug is INDICATED FOR or its DOSAGE rather than its interaction/contraindication/warning sections?"* That is the nearest thing in the repo. But it is scoped to **descriptive-vs-safety section class**, on **3 danger queries**, and asks nothing about whether a document's *content* carries an inference.
    - **`scripts/citation_truth_check.py` genuinely checks support** — sentence-level claim vs cited abstract, verdicts `same`/`reversed`/`not_directional`/`abstract_silent`. But it is **not a gate** (a Task-A QA harness whose output has never been committed), it is **PubMed-only by construction**, and 🔴 **its `extract_pmid` silently MIS-EXTRACTS the other source types** — measured 2026-08-23: a DailyMed `source_id` yields PMID **`84432`** (trailing setid digits) and a TFDA one yields **`057803`**. So on R12's three DailyMed citations it would fetch three unrelated PubMed abstracts and judge against those. **Filed as flagged-not-fixed.**
  - **THE MECHANISM the reviewer identified — and it is NOT the resolver bug.** `R12` **names no drug at all**. Retrieval can therefore only fetch anticoagulation-*adjacent* documents, and the generator organises whatever arrives into a complete-looking answer. ⚠️ **This is a different shape from the resolver's exact/base mutual exclusivity** (`resolver.py:102-108`), which requires **both** drugs to be named and drops one. Wrong-support needs **neither** drug named. The two do not share a fix.
  - **⚠️ N=1. ONE OBSERVED INSTANCE. NO MEASUREMENT OF RATE EXISTS** — and none can be inferred from this review (see the review entry below for why 24 units cannot separate rare from common). Nothing here claims a frequency.
  - **Why [HONESTY] and why [P1] — argued, not defaulted.** **[HONESTY]** because this is **user-facing**: the product's central positioning is citation-mandatory grounding, and a reader seeing three DailyMed citations under drug-pair guidance reasonably infers those labels support it. They do not. That is the product telling the user something untrue about its own evidence — unlike a runner misreporting to a developer, which was re-classed `[OTHER]` on 2026-08-24. **[P1]** because it is invisible to every gate, sits on a **danger-adjacent** surface (drug-pair bleeding risk), and defeats the product's core claim rather than degrading it. ⚠️ **Counter-argument, recorded rather than suppressed:** it is **N=1**, no rate is known, and the reviewer found nothing clinically wrong with the answer's *content* — a founder could reasonably rate this **[P2]** until a rate exists.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-23, founder-directed human review of the 24-unit answer-layer pack.

- [DONE] **[P2 · answer-layer wrong-drug review — the FIRST measurement of this axis in the project; 9 scoreable units read by a reviewer with clinical background, 2026-08-23] Zero wrong-drug answers, INCLUDING all four deliberately-loaded resolver-misfire cases — with the caveats that bound it stated alongside, not beneath**
  *(body → docs/archive/tech_debt_done.md, archived verbatim 2026-08-27 — C3 rule; heading above kept verbatim)*

- [HONESTY] **[P1 · retrieval measurement / premise integrity — five read-only recons + TWO founder-authorized live runs, 2026-08-22/23; FINDINGS ONLY, nothing re-scoped] 🔴 主線 A #1 (reserved seat + ownership interception) was scoped on a premise the measurements now contradict: THE SEAT TARGETS A STAGE WHERE DOCUMENTS DO NOT DIE. The relevance filter removes ~7× what the final slice removes, and the cut the seat's stage-1 protection guards NEVER FIRES on real Research queries**
  - **⚠️ THIS ENTRY RECORDS EVIDENCE. IT DOES NOT REVISE A DECISION.** The 主線 A scope and the S1+I1 choice are **founder rulings and they stand**. What is filed here is that several premises those rulings rested on are now measured false. **The founder re-sequences; this entry only makes sure the evidence survives the session.** Nothing below re-scopes, retracts, or re-rates any ruling.
  - **⏸️ 2026-08-26 — THE RE-SEQUENCING HAPPENED (founder Ruling B — `docs/batons/positioning_audit_20260823.md` §3):** 主線 A #1 (reserved seat) is **PARKED, premise-refuted**, per the BACKLOG entry's own *"founder re-sequences"* clause (that entry carries the matching ⏸️ annotation). **The successor line is the relevance-filter question** — *which 60% does the filter drop* — carried by the `_filter_by_relevance` [P2] entry below, promoted to schedulable by the same ruling. This entry's evidence stands unchanged as the basis of that ruling; annotation only — no re-scope, no re-rate, no class change.
  - **① THE FILTER IS THE DOMINANT LOSS STAGE — and it is the ONLY finding in this line where a synthetic-population result survived contact with real queries.** Measured 2026-08-23 on the **real golden Research set** (25 cases × N=3 = **75 in-process `retrieve()` calls**, zero errors, zero fail-open, zero retrieval short-circuits): the filter takes **13.85 in → 5.32 out, removing 8.53 documents = 60.7%** (median 62.5%). `seat_v2`'s 20 **synthetic** queries measured **11.3 → 4.7 = 58.4%**. **They agree within 2.3 points.**
    - **Removal rate as a DISTRIBUTION, not a mean** — a mean would hide whether the filter usually removes a little and occasionally everything. It does neither: **0% kept-all 1 run · 1–25% 2 · 26–50% 21 · 51–75% 41 · 76–99% 7 · 100% killed-all 3.** **64% of runs sit in the 51–100% band.**
    - **THE FULL LADDER, from production's OWN logs** (consistent denominator: the 72 runs reaching `:291`): merge **16.96** → dedup **13.82** → **filter 5.54** → pre-slice **5.54** → post-slice **4.36**. **Filter −8.28 · final slice −1.18 · rerank+composite+collapse 0.00.**
    - **🔑 MEASURED WITH ZERO `api/` CHANGE.** `api/rag/retriever.py:531` **already emits** `"Relevance filter: %d -> %d documents kept"` at INFO in production, and `:29` is `logging.getLogger(__name__)`. A handler attached from **outside** `api/` reads it. `:225` (dedup), `:75` (CutExempt), `:527` (FilterExempt) and `:291` (pre-slice) are likewise already emitted — **most of the attrition ladder was readable all along and had never been read.** No committed probe had ever harvested `:531`.
  - **② 🔴 WHAT THE SEAT TARGETS IS NOT WHERE DOCUMENTS DIE.**
    - **17 of 20 `seat_v2` queries ENTER the pipeline with ≥5 documents** (mean pre-seat multi-source pool **17.7**; only 1 starts at zero). The sub-5 final size is created **downstream of retrieval**, not by the `min_score` floor the seat bypasses.
    - **The `[:max_results*4]` cut at `:232` NEVER BOUND on the real set:** max `dedup_unique` was **19** across 75 runs, `filter_in == dedup_unique` in **75/75**, and **`_cut_exemption` (`:237`) fired 0 times.**
    - **CONSEQUENCE for the P1-vs-P2 question, recorded NOT answered:** the stage-1 protection `seat_v2` modelled as **P1 is not merely free in production — it is INERT on this population**, because the cut it guards never fires. The earlier finding *"P1 is free, so the question is P2-or-nothing"* is therefore sharper than stated: on these queries P1 would protect against nothing at all. **The question itself remains the founder's.**
    - **③ PRODUCTION IS ALREADY RESCUING THE EXACT DOCUMENT CLASS THE SEAT WOULD INJECT, AT THE STAGE WHERE THE LOSS ACTUALLY HAPPENS.** `[FilterExempt]` (`:527`) fired on **21 of 75 runs (28%)**, re-adding **31 documents** across **15 distinct source_ids**, all DailyMed whitelisted safety LOINCs. Without it mean-out would be **4.91** instead of 5.32. *(Arithmetic verified in source: `:529` appends the exemption BEFORE `:531` logs, so the logged out-count already includes the re-adds.)*
  - **④ THE REACH CEILING = 10/30 = 33%, AND IT IS REACH, NOT BENEFIT.** Hand-mapped over 30 queries (R01–R20 + the 6 canaries + 4 known misfires) **by reading each query, not by resolver output — a judgement, stated as one**: **20/30 name a drug CLASS or no drug at all**, so no resolver and no `k` can engage; 1 names a drug absent from the corpus as its own moiety (lithium); **10/30 name a drug present in the corpus owning whitelisted safety docs.** ⚠️ **Upper bound only** — several of those 10 (R01, R10, R11) already retrieve their safety sections today without any seat, so realised benefit is strictly lower.
  - **⑤ THE RESOLVER IS NOT SEAT-READY, AND NEITHER BUILDER IS A SUPERSET OF THE OTHER.** Measured over the same 30 queries:
    - **A (`resolve_key_set` — exact + base→salt):** reach **10/30 = 33%**; **clean 6/30 = 20%** after removing 3 wholly-wrong misfires and 1 partial. Handles the **salt gap** B cannot — heparin (R06, R15).
    - **B (`build_key_sets` — alias + setid + rxcui):** reach **8/30 = 27%**; **clean 6/30 = 20%** (1 wholly-wrong, 1 partial). Closes **aspirin** (R08, R13) — `{ASPIRIN}` owns **0** safety docs while `ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID` owns 3. ⚠️ **That win is 100% a hand-written `ALIAS_GROUPS` seed.** Its two *generalising* rules join **0 pairs** on the shipped corpus, and **`rxcui` is empty in all 4,608 docs**. There is **no aspirin-shaped case beyond aspirin**.
    - **They agree on only 4 of their 6 clean hits** (R01, R10, R11, metformin_moa); A uniquely gets R06/R15, B uniquely gets R08/R13. *(⚠️ A "27% vs 20% correct" split circulated in the baton; re-derived here it does **not** reproduce — **both land at 6/30 = 20% clean**, only reach differs.)*
    - **🔴 CALCIUM.** The **only** corpus moiety that is also a salt-suffix word — and it is **Calcium Gluconate** (setid `4c635f60-7868-46d5-b932-d5cf8fc84432`), an IV electrolyte. `"calcium channel blocker mechanism"` seats its Contraindications, Drug Interactions and Warnings. **BOTH builders misfire** (A seats 4, B seats 3). The canary set contains **no calcium query**, so the amended gate would pass while this is live.
    - **🔴 `exact` AND `base` ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE, NOT ADDITIVE.** `resolver.py:102-108` returns on the first non-empty exact match **before** reaching the base branch at `:110`. Measured: `"warfarin and aspirin together"` → `via=exact, keys={ASPIRIN}` — **warfarin is silently dropped.** Live in golden **R13**. This is the **drug-pair interaction query — the shape most central to this product.**
    - **base→salt misfires on ordinary English:** `SODIUM` → `SODIUM ACETATE` and `POTASSIUM` → `POTASSIUM ACETATE` **confirmed firing** on a dietary-sodium and a potassium-supplement query. A further set — `CAFFEINE`, `THIAMINE`, `BARIUM`, `FERRIC`, `GALLIUM`, `PYRIDOXINE` — is **flagged as a judgement call the founder has not made**; all 232 non-moiety bases are listed in the artifact, unfiltered.
    - **⚠️ DIRECTION INVERSION, AND IT IS NOT IN THE DOCSTRING.** `resolver.py:24-31` records the false-positive mode and reasons that a larger key set makes **a GATE less strict**. Used as a **SEAT** the same misfire **actively injects wrong-object content**. The documented mitigation reasoning does not transfer to the new surface — **a CLAUDE.md Rule 19 shape**, on a module written for the gate and now proposed for the seat.
  - **⑥ THE `5` IS AN UNEXAMINED DEFAULT.** Four live `5`s on the request path; **three trace to `631b6e5` (2026-02-13) — the initial commit, when the product was named *MediNotes*, whose entire message is one line naming neither retrieval nor citations.** The other two rode inside unrelated multi-item feature commits. **No commit message, code comment, ADR, PRD section, or founder ruling anywhere gives a reason for the value.** The undocumented `Reranker(top_k=8)` between pool and slice (`retriever.py:134`, a bare call) has the same provenance. `ndcg_at_k(labels, r_corpus, k)` **is parameterised on k and has only ever been called with 5**, so `mean_ndcg5 = 0.3729` and `wrong_object_at_rank1 = 0.20` are **single-point values with no comparator.** **Cross-ref the `BACKLOG.md` entry headed *"Research pool budget — `top_k=5` cuts safety sections sitting at composite rank 6"***, which scoped a 5-vs-6-vs-7 sweep three times, never ran it, and invalidated its own rank evidence as pre-c1.
  - **⑦ POST-c1 POOL SIZE — re-ran the committed harvest 2026-08-23** (`tests/results/_poolsize_harvest.py`, **unmodified**; 16m34s, 75 calls, **zero exclusions**). Pre-c1 `{0:1, 1:6, 2:1, 3:5, 4:18, 5:44}` mean **4.20** → post-c1 `{0:3, 1:6, 2:5, 3:6, 4:15, 5:40}` mean **3.92**. Below-5 **41% → 47%**; **53% still hit the ceiling of 5.**
    - **"Most Research queries end below 5" remains CONTRADICTED post-c1.** Closer to a coin-flip, still not "most".
    - **🔴 TB02 accounts for roughly half the mean shift, and it is the measurement finally seeing through a known-empty source — NOT a regression.** TB02 (`冠脂妥剝半吃藥效會有影響嗎`) went `[4,5,4] → [0,0,1]`. Its pre-c1 pool was **11 of 13 cited docs from `local`** — the 690 documents this file records as **100% EMPTY STUBS**. Its 4–5 pool size was **fake fullness**. Excluding TB02 the drift is mean 4.194 → 4.069 (**−0.125**). Corpus-wide, `local` was **26 of 315 cited docs (8%)** pre-c1, in 15 of 74 runs.
    - **⚠️ CONFOUND THAT CANNOT BE REMOVED:** ~4 weeks of live PubMed/FDA drift sits between the two runs and is **not separable from c1 without a same-day A/B**, which is not retrospectively possible. The original harvest measured mean run-to-run turnover **0.423**, so the −0.125 residual is well inside known churn.
  - **⑧ 🔴 THE MECHANISM THAT DECIDED ALL OF THE ABOVE — POPULATION, NOT METHOD. Recorded because it recurred THREE times in four days.** A finding measured on `seat_v2`'s **synthetic `{drug}+{template}` queries** does not generalise to Research traffic — but it sometimes does, and the method never told us which:
    - **pool size — FAILED to generalise.** `13/20 = 65%` of synthetic queries end below 5. The real golden Research set is **41% pre-c1 / 47% post-c1** — a minority both times. *(The 13/20 figure is **TRUE for its own population** and is marked, not deleted; it was never written into a repo file, so this bullet is its record.)*
    - **resolver reach — FAILED to generalise.** Synthetic single-drug queries resolve by construction; **20 of 30 real queries name a class or no drug at all.**
    - **filter attrition — HELD.** 58.4% synthetic vs **60.7%** real, agreeing within 2.3 points.
    - **The method was identical in all three. Only the population differed, and only the population decided.** ⚠️ **A synthetic-set result is a hypothesis about Research traffic, never a measurement of it** — and the confirming case is the one that makes this dangerous, because it teaches the wrong lesson if read alone.
    - **⚠️ AND A BETTER WITNESS WAS ALREADY COMMITTED, AND WAS NOT CONSULTED.** [`docs/poolsize_distribution_harvest.md`](docs/poolsize_distribution_harvest.md) (2026-07-28) records **75 real `retriever.retrieve()` runs over the golden Research set** and has been in the repo the whole time. The 13/20 claim was carried for days while the artifact that refutes it sat committed. **CHECK before generalising any harness result: does a committed real-query artifact already cover this?**
  - **Evidence:** ✅ **COMMITTED 2026-08-24** — [`tests/probes/retrieval_attrition/`](tests/probes/retrieval_attrition/), six diagnostics + the runner + the irreplaceable pre-c1 raw, with a README that leads on **population** and states per artifact what it does NOT support. *(~~`tests/results/`, gitignored~~ — moved, not regenerated.)* **Surfaced:** 2026-08-22/23.

They survive only as comments in two scripts in a session-scoped OS temp directory, which vanishes with the session**
