- [OTHER] **[P3 · public-page / i18n — surfaced while resolving the c1 Task-3.7.2 discrepancy 2026-08-03] A sitemap-listed public SEO page returns a RAW FastAPI JSON 404 to a zh-TW visitor — and `?locale=zh` works while `?locale=zh-TW` does not**
  - **Measured:** `/explore/metformin-contraindications-renal` → `?locale=en` **200** · no param **200** · **`?locale=zh` 200** · **`?locale=zh-TW` 404 `{"detail":"Not found."}`**. Reproduced on both `vela-ai-medical.fly.dev` and `vela.an-tho.com`.
  - **Cause:** `explore_renderer.render_explore_page` (`:147-163`) queries a row keyed by **(slug, locale)** and only an `en` row was authored. The perverse part: **`zh` is NOT a first-class locale so it degrades to `en` and succeeds, while `zh-TW` IS first-class, is therefore honoured, and then fails the row lookup.** The supported locale is the one that breaks.
  - **Why it matters:** the response is **raw JSON**, not a styled 404 or an English fallback — a poor result for a public SEO surface, and Vela's primary market is zh-TW. The sitemap only advertises `?locale=en`, which bounds crawler exposure, but an in-app zh-TW user navigating there sees `{"detail":"Not found."}`.
  - **Fix-direction (not scoped):** fall back to the `en` row when a locale-specific row is absent (matching the `zh` behaviour), or return a styled localized 404. **Not caused by c1; unrelated to the deprecation.**
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-03.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · measurement-instrument drift — CAUGHT DURING the c1 ship 2026-07-29, FIXED at the default] NINE measurement harnesses construct `HybridRetriever()` bare, so they matched production BY ACCIDENT — the moment production changed a source flag they would have kept measuring a DEAD SOURCE**
  - **How it was caught:** the **first** section-aware danger-path re-gate for c1 returned a clean 0 violations — and its log said **`✅ Vector store loaded: 690 documents`**. `scripts/dailymed_danger_path_verify.py:181` calls `HybridRetriever()` with no arguments, inheriting the library default `enable_local=True`, so it measured the **pre-ship** configuration. **That result was discarded, not reported.** It was caught by reading the harness's own startup log rather than trusting its verdict.
  - **Nine bare call sites:** `dailymed_danger_path_verify` · `direction_shadow_eval` · `pathb_recall_probe` · `pathb_scope_probe` · `question_neutralization_eval` · `retrieval_recall_check` · `source_weight_activation_verify` · `source_weight_local_tier_sweep` · `source_weight_shadow_eval`.
  - **✅ FIXED AT THE DEFAULT, not the call site** — `HybridRetriever.__init__` `enable_local: bool = False` (`api/rag/retriever.py:96`), so the class is closed rather than one instance. Call sites that genuinely want the corpus pass `enable_local=True` explicitly and are unaffected (**124 tests pass**).
  - **🔁 FIFTH instance of the "instrument blind to its own target" class** — and the **first caught during the change** rather than months later. See the class table on the [P2 · founder-decision] local-tier entry below.
  - **⚠️ THE `enable_local` DEFAULT FIX CLOSES THE PARAMETER, NOT THE CLASS — stated plainly, correcting an over-claim in the c1 build record.** The class is: **nine harnesses each construct their own retriever instead of sharing production's construction path**, so **any** future divergence (a new flag, a new source, a changed weight, a changed call argument) will silently re-open with **no signal**. Flipping one default fixed one instance of the symptom.
  - **✅ FULL DIVERGENCE SWEEP RUN 2026-07-30 (all 9 harnesses × every construction parameter AND every `retrieve()` argument, vs `api/server.py:449`/`:828`). Result — one clean, two real:**
    - **(1) CONSTRUCTION PARAMETERS: ZERO divergence. A real result, so it is stated as one.** After the default flip, production's explicit construction (`local_threshold=0.6 · enable_local=False · enable_pubmed=True · enable_fda=True · enable_tfda=True`, `enable_dailymed` defaulted) is **byte-equivalent to the constructor defaults**, so all nine bare-construction harnesses match production on **all six** parameters. ⚠️ **This equivalence is COINCIDENTAL and load-bearing** — the moment production overrides any flag again, all nine silently diverge.
    - **(2) ⚠️ `retrieve()` ARGUMENTS DIVERGE — `source_weight_active`.** Signature default is **`False`** (`retriever.py:147`); **production passes `True`** (`server.py:827` reads `SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE`, ON in prod since v200). Harnesses that OMIT it measure the **composite reorder DISABLED** — a ranking path production has not used since **2026-07-06**. **Accidental** (the harness intends production behaviour): `direction_shadow_eval.py:78` · `question_neutralization_eval.py:58` · `tests/results/_neut_probe.py:42`. **Deliberate** (the harness's *purpose* is ON-vs-OFF or shadow-sink measurement, so this is correct): `source_weight_activation_verify.py:66` (OFF arm; `:63` is the ON arm) · `source_weight_shadow_eval.py:57` · `source_weight_local_tier_sweep.py:60`. **Matching production:** `dailymed_danger_path_verify.py:134` passes `source_weight_active=True` ✅.
    - **✅ NO RECORDED GATE RESULT IS INVALIDATED — checked, not assumed.** The only *gate* among the nine is `dailymed_danger_path_verify`, which matches production. And the three accidental cases (`direction_shadow_eval`, `question_neutralization_eval`, `_neut_probe`) were all run **2026-06-17, before v200 activated on 2026-07-06** — at the time they ran they *did* match production. **The exposure is on FUTURE runs, not on the historical record.**
    - **(3) ⚠️ TWO HARNESSES DO NOT CALL `retrieve()` AT ALL — they HAND-MIRROR its stages** through private methods: `pathb_recall_probe.py:66` and `retrieval_recall_check.py:55-57` call `_search_local` / `_search_pubmed` / `_search_fda` directly. **They therefore model a THREE-source pipeline — no TFDA, no DailyMed** — while production fans out to five. This is a deeper divergence than a wrong argument: it re-implements the thing it measures, and drifts every time `retrieve()` changes.
    - **(3b) …and it now fails QUIET, not loud.** With `enable_local=False`, `self.vector_store is None`, so `_search_local` raises `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'search'` — but `retriever.py:593-601` wraps the body in `except Exception` and returns `[]`, logging only `WARNING Local search error: …`. **Verified live.** So those two harnesses now silently measure **zero local documents** behind a warning that reads like a transient search failure. **Production is unaffected** (`:191` guards the call on `enable_local`), so this is harness-only exposure — but the broad `except` converting a programming error into a warning is worth recording on its own.
  - **🔁 THIRD RECURRENCE OF THE MIRROR-NOT-THE-THING DEFECT — appended 2026-08-23, NOT re-rated.** The `(3)` bullet above records harnesses that hand-mirror `retrieve()`'s stages. That shape has now cost three distinct things in the 主線 A line, and they are listed together because the pattern is the finding:
    - **(i) `seat_v2` seat-survival needs RE-MEASURING after any build.** Its own `method_per_stage.mirror_caveat` says so: *"Stages are a MIRROR of `retrieve():208-292`, not `retrieve()` itself… only the dedup/sort/slice arithmetic is mirrored line-for-line. This is the instrument-blindness class of TECH_DEBT #8, unavoidable without instrumenting `api/`, and declared rather than hidden."* Declared honestly — and still a debt.
    - **(ii) THE `13/20 QUERIES END BELOW 5` FIGURE CAME FROM THE MIRROR AND WAS OVERTURNED.** It was reported as a Research-wide property; the real-set figure is **41% pre-c1 / 47% post-c1**. See the premise-integrity entry above.
    - **(iii) AN OFFLINE MIRROR WAS THE OPTION EXPLICITLY REJECTED for measuring the relevance filter — on these grounds** — and the alternative that was used instead needed **no `api/` change at all**, because production already logs the pair at `retriever.py:531`. **The mirror was not the only way to avoid touching `api/`; reading what production already emits was.** That option had not previously been looked for.
  - **📋 PROPOSED (do NOT build here; founder call): a single shared production-construction helper** — e.g. `api/rag/retriever.build_production_retriever()` used by `api/server.py` **and** by every harness, so a harness cannot construct a different retriever than production without saying so explicitly. Harnesses that deliberately vary a parameter would pass an explicit override, making the variation **visible in the diff** instead of implicit in a default. This makes divergence **structurally impossible** rather than repeatedly caught by luck. It touches nine scripts plus the server wiring and belongs in its own baton. **A second, cheaper half:** the same helper should own the `retrieve()` argument defaults, since surface (2) shows construction parity alone is not enough.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · TFDA data staleness — surfaced by the fly-214 citation gate 2026-07-29] The TFDA indication corpus is built from a PINNED snapshot and cites licences that EXPIRE; 有效日期 was DROPPED at build so no runtime staleness check is possible. The DEEP-LINK anchor decays FASTER than the document**
  - **The clock:** the first TFDA citation ever browser-eyeballed carries 有效日期 **116-01-18 = 2027-01-18** — under six months out. id=37 (未註銷藥品許可證資料集) is *active as of the snapshot* (`data/tfda/snapshot_20260630/`, pulled 2026-06-30). **Licences expire; a pinned snapshot does not.** This is an accuracy/honesty defect that **arrives on a schedule rather than from a code change** — no commit will ever cause it and no regression test will ever catch it.
  - **Measured** (all 10,941 docs joined to the snapshot on 許可證字號; **17,881 distinct cited licences, 0 unmatched, 0 missing dates**; ref 2026-07-29): **71 (0.4%) ALREADY EXPIRED** · <3mo 298 · 3–6mo 533 · 6–12mo 1,245 → **cumulative 2.1% at 3 months, 5.0% at 6, 12.0% at 12, 36.3% at 24.** By document (stale only when **all** its licences are): **34/10,941 (0.31%)** today; forward decay 1.5% / 3.7% / 8.9% at 3 / 6 / 12 months.
  - **⚠️ SUB-FINDING — representative-licence selection is not expiry-aware, so the link rots first.** `source_id` + `url` are built from ONE representative licence: **40 docs (0.4%) already deep-link to an expired licence; 476 (4.4%) to one expiring within 6 months.** **1,223 docs (11.2%) chose a representative that expires SOONER than one of its own siblings.** **8,748 (80.0%)** have only one licence, so no sibling exists. **The gate's own citation is an instance:** 冠脂妥膜衣錠5毫克 has **12** licences spanning 2027-01-18 → **2031-07-18**, and the deep-link anchors on **the earliest-expiring of the twelve**.
  - **有效日期 is NOT in the corpus.** Fields are `content · source_type · source_id · title · url · credibility · doc_type · drug_name · licenses · product_count`; **0/10,941** documents mention 有效日期 in content. **Therefore no runtime staleness check is currently possible** — the date is only recoverable by re-joining the snapshot ZIP.
  - **The build filtered once, and that filter is already decaying:** `_meta.stats` = `dropped_expired_or_no_date: 1069` at `ref_date: 2026/06/30`. **71 licences valid at build have expired in the 29 days since.**
  - **🔧 SCOPED 2026-07-29 — the cheap independent win, ready to fold into whatever ships next: an expiry-aware representative-licence SORT KEY.**
    - **What:** in `scripts/build_tfda_indication_corpus.py`, when a document's licence group has more than one 許可證字號, pick the representative by **latest 有效日期** instead of the current selection. **One sort key.**
    - **Why it is worth doing on its own:** **40 documents already deep-link to an EXPIRED licence** and **1,223 (11.2%) picked a representative expiring sooner than one of their own siblings**. The founder's own gate citation is an instance — **冠脂妥膜衣錠5毫克 has 12 licences spanning 2027-01-18 → 2031-07-18 and the link anchors on the earliest of the twelve.** Fixing the key buys ~4 extra years of link life on that document alone.
    - **Bounded by construction:** **8,748 documents (80.0%) have exactly one licence**, so nothing changes for four fifths of the corpus — the change touches only the 20% with a choice, and can only move a representative to a *later*-expiring one.
    - **⚠️ Cost gate — check before scoping further:** changing the representative changes `source_id`, `url` and possibly `title`. **If content stays byte-identical, NO re-embed is needed** — v196 already proved that path (*"corpus content byte-identical → NO re-embed"*). **If the representative name is interpolated into `content`, it is a re-embed.** Determine which before estimating. Cross-ref the v197 rep-name/rep-字號 mismatch fix, which is the same surface.
    - **NOT built here.**
  - **PROPOSED (not scoped):** id=37 is a **single anonymous bulk-ZIP GET** (OGDL v1.0, ~4.4 MB) and the manifest already says `"Pin + monthly re-pull"` with `pulled_at: 2026-06-30` — **⏰ SO THE FIRST MONTHLY RE-PULL IS DUE NOW (as of 2026-07-29, 29 days elapsed). This is an OPERATIONAL action for the founder, not a code task** — the pull is one anonymous GET; the only question is whether to re-embed (see the cost gate above). Open questions are cadence (monthly outruns a 2.1%/quarter decay) and whether a diff should gate anything — **the expensive part is the re-embed, not the pull**, and v196 proved a content-byte-identical refresh path exists (deep-link ship, no re-embed). **Cheapest independent win, orthogonal to cadence: make representative-licence selection prefer the LATEST-expiring licence** (one sort key in the build script; immediately fixes the 11.2%). Carrying 有效日期 into the record would enable a runtime check — a schema change, not free.
  - **Cross-ref [ADR 007](docs/decisions/007-tfda-open-data-grounding.md)** and the 在地差異 **(e)** item *"the authority URLs need periodic review"* (BACKLOG) — **same family, different data layer**; they should probably share one "external data goes stale on a schedule" mechanism rather than each growing its own cron.
  - **Copyright note (recorded so it is not re-litigated):** the `mcp.fda.gov.tw` page carries a site-content copyright notice, which is **distinct** from the corpus's provenance — the corpus is the **OGDL v1.0 open data (id=37)**, and **deep-linking is not reproduction**. Consistent with, and independent support for, the existing **a2 DEFER** reasoning. **No action needed.**
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-29. **Evidence:** [`docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md`](docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md) Finding 6. **NOT fixed — measurement only.**

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · retrieval-source integrity / honesty — surfaced by the citation deep-link baton 2026-07-29] openFDA is STRUCTURALLY INERT in Research: its query construction can never match, so the advertised "5th source" contributes NOTHING — and its URL is a hard-coded homepage, which imposes a HARD ORDERING CONSTRAINT on any fix**
  - **The defect (`api/data_sources/fda.py:127`):**
    ```python
    search_query = f'openfda.brand_name:"{query}" OR openfda.generic_name:"{query}"'
    ```
    In the **Research** path `query` is the **full rewritten multi-word string** (e.g. `"metformin pharmacokinetics renal impairment"`), so this asks openFDA for a *brand name* equal to an entire sentence. It cannot match → HTTP 404 / `no_results`. **Measured:** two forced retrievals with `source_filter=[SourceType.FDA]` returned **`status=no_results`, 0 documents**; live server logs show the 404s directly. This also independently explains the pair-aware Phase-1 finding that **openFDA was cited 0/18** — it was never *retrieved*, not out-ranked.
    *(Verify is unaffected: it calls `search_drug_labels(drug)` with a **single drug name**, which matches correctly. The defect is specific to Research's rewritten-query fan-out at `api/rag/retriever.py:185`.)*
  - **📉 HONESTY IMPACT — the advertised source count is effectively 4, not 5.** Research fans out to local · PubMed · **openFDA** · TFDA · DailyMed, and openFDA contributes nothing. **CROSS-REF the existing `[P2]` "Full-site DailyMed over-claim sweep" — same honesty family:** that entry exists because we described a data source we had not integrated; this is the mirror case — a source we *do* wire up, advertise, and pay latency for, which returns nothing.
  - **🔴 HARD ORDERING CONSTRAINT — READ BEFORE FIXING. THE URL MUST BE FIXED FIRST, OR IN THE SAME CHANGE.**
    - `api/data_sources/fda.py:31-33` returns a **hard-coded `https://labels.fda.gov/`** for **every** label — a generic homepage, not the cited document.
    - That value **PASSES `isUsableSourceUrl()`** (`components/CitationPanel.tsx`), which only requires an absolute `http(s)` URL and deliberately does **not** judge provenance.
    - **Therefore: repairing the query WITHOUT repairing the URL would immediately start shipping false-provenance "View source" links** — openFDA documents would begin reaching top_k for the first time, each linking to a homepage that implies it is the source. That is the "FDA Label Analysis" honesty defect family (v199/v201) re-introduced by a *recall* fix.
    - **Required order:** (1) give openFDA citations a real per-document URL (openFDA responses carry `openfda.spl_set_id` / `id`, so a DailyMed-style `drugInfo.cfm?setid=` deep-link is constructible — **verify before relying on it**), THEN (2) fix the query construction. Or both in one change. **Never (2) alone.**
  - **🔗 COUPLING to the wrong-drug citation defect (record only — do NOT reorder the pending baton):** wrong-drug citations occur precisely where **nothing on-target exists** — **128/1038 (12.3%)** corpus moieties lack any safety section, including **ASPIRIN, IBUPROFEN, NAPROXEN**. Phase 1 measured that **openFDA has labels for 15/15** of those sampled, with safety text on **14/15**. So a working openFDA path is a plausible **UPSTREAM** fix for the same defect: give retrieval something on-target to return, instead of filtering out the neighbour it currently returns. **This is recorded as a relationship, not a plan.** The pending wrong-drug baton (citation-integrity guard, `docs/wrong_drug_citation_severity.md` §6) is **not** reordered or rescoped by this note — **the founder sequences.**
  - **Why [P2] and not [P1]:** nothing false is asserted today *because of this defect* — the source is simply silent, and Research degrades to the four working sources. It is not [P3] because it is (a) an honesty exposure on an advertised source, (b) wasted latency and API budget on every Research query, and (c) a plausible upstream fix for a defect that IS user-visible. **Not [P0/P1]** — but note the ordering constraint above turns a naive fix INTO a [P1]-class honesty defect, which is why it is written in bold.
  - **✅ OBSERVATIONAL SUPPORT ADDED 2026-07-29 (fly-214 prod human-eye gate):** the gate's row 5 looked specifically for an openFDA chip. **No LINKED "FDA" chip appeared in any query** — every FDA chip observed was a `local` document (correctly rendering no link). So the structural-inertness conclusion, previously proven only by code inspection plus two forced `source_filter` retrievals, now also **holds under observation on prod**. It remains true that **no rendered openFDA citation has ever been produced**, so its URL value is still proven by inspection (a compile-time constant), not by a rendered instance.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-29. **Evidence:** `docs/citation_deeplink_fix.md` Task 1b; correction note in `docs/research_openfda_fallback_phase1.md`; gate row 5 in `docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md` §0.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · shadow-instrument integrity — surfaced by the pool-size harvest 2026-07-28] `RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to the most one-sided pools — the exact shape it exists to detect. Its bias direction UNDERSTATES the phenomenon, which biases the pending enforce decision toward "no enforcement needed"**
  - **Mechanism (read-only code check; nothing changed or enabled):** `api/server.py:571-572` returns early when `len(sources) < 2`. `sources` comes from `pool_sources_from_documents` (`api/services/retrieval_refusal.py:69-79`), which counts **PubMed documents ONLY** (`:73-74` skips every non-PubMed `source_type`) that also carry a PMID and non-empty content (`:77`). Therefore:
    - a **`pool_size = 1`** pool can **never** reach `rr.assess()`;
    - the gate is on **PubMed-doc count, not pool size** — a 2-doc pool of **1 PubMed + 1 DailyMed** is also short-circuited.
  - **📉 DIRECTION OF THE BIAS (the part that matters):** the censored cases are the **most extreme one-sided pools** — a 1-document pool is maximally one-sided. So the shadow's dataset systematically **omits its own worst cases** and therefore **UNDERSTATES** how often one-sidedness occurs. **Any read of that data understates the problem, and a decision taken on it is biased toward "no enforcement needed."** Quantified scale from the same-day harvest (`docs/poolsize_distribution_harvest.md`): **`pool_size ≤ 2` on 11% of runs (8/75), clustered in 3/25 cases.**
  - **⚠️ THIS BLOCKS A PENDING DECISION.** `STATE.md:13` records the reversal-defense **enforce/A2 step as deferred "pending real B2B shadow traffic"** since **v181 (2026-06-18)** — i.e. waiting on exactly this dataset. The decision has been waiting ~6 weeks on an instrument that cannot see its own target population.
  - **🔁 SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME CLASS — cross-ref the recorded `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` censoring (fly 207, [P1] entry below):** *"the shadow record is built only when `_sw_shadow_sink` is non-empty … it omits them non-randomly."* Both are **a shadow instrument whose sample non-randomly excludes the population it was built to measure**. Two independent occurrences make this a pattern worth a standing check, not a one-off.
    - **Kinship with CLAUDE.md Rule 17** (*tests must verify intent, not just behavior*): a shadow that records only the cases it happens to reach verifies **behavior** ("we logged what we saw") while failing **intent** ("we measured how often the bad shape occurs"). Rule 17's question — *"what business rule breaks if this fails?"* — applied to a shadow instrument becomes *"what would this instrument be unable to tell me even in principle?"*
  - **PROPOSED, NOT SCOPED (two options — founder to choose; do NOT treat as a plan):**
    - **(i) Widen the gate** so small/one-sided pools are assessed rather than skipped. Needs care: `assess()` compares sources against each other, so a 1-source input may not be meaningful to the existing prompt — this could be a genuine design constraint rather than an oversight, and that must be checked before calling it a bug.
    - **(ii) Re-read the existing prod shadow logs WITH the censoring known**, treating recorded rates as a **lower bound** and reasoning about the missing stratum separately. Cheaper, no code change, but cannot recover data that was never written.
  - **Nothing was enabled, changed, or fetched.** Prod shadow logs were **not** read this baton.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-28. **Evidence:** `docs/poolsize_distribution_harvest.md`; code refs above.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · DailyMed corpus coverage / honesty — surfaced by the pair-aware probe 2026-07-27] The Research DailyMed corpus has an UNMEASURED common-drug safety-coverage hole: 36.9% of reference labels dropped at build; 11% of 47 commonly-prescribed generics unreachable for a safety-section lever — while we present DailyMed as the 5th Research retrieval source**
  - **What (from the corpus's own `_meta.stats`, `data/dailymed/label_docs.json`):** `backbone_reference_labels` **1908** → `labels_with_sections` **1203** → **`labels_dropped_zero_sections_or_fetch_fail` = 705 = 36.9%**. Resolve-stage provenance: `miss 627 · original 952 · normalized_recall 135 · mono_rescue 116 · dropped_combo 78`.
  - **Reachability measured against 47 commonly-prescribed generics: 5/47 = 11% unreachable** for a safety-section lever — but the causes are **FOUR different things and must NOT be closed together**:
    - **AMIODARONE — ✅ the only genuine unexplained loss (1/47).** `AMIODARONE HCL` **is** in the TFDA mono set (i.e. in scope) yet is **absent from the entire 1038-moiety corpus**. A high-interaction antiarrhythmic.
    - **PREDNISONE — ❌ NOT a defect: a Taiwan market difference.** Family (substring) matching shows TFDA carries it **combo-only**; the mono forms are MEPREDNISONE / METHYLPREDNISONE, and the **PREDNISOLONE** family has 7 TFDA mono entries / 3 in-corpus **with** safety sections. Taiwan prescribes prednisolone predominantly.
    - **INSULIN — ❌ NOT a build defect: a scope boundary.** **0** hits in TFDA mono, TFDA combo, and the corpus — the TFDA 西藥許可證 backbone (id=37) carries no biologics at all.
    - **ASPIRIN + NAPROXEN — ⚠️ present in the corpus with NO safety sections** (OTC Drug-Facts label shape). ASPIRIN's safety content exists but under a **different moiety key** (see the synonym item below); NAPROXEN has no alternative key — a genuine content gap.
  - **⚠️ ATTRIBUTION LIMIT (Rule 18):** the `705` counter **conflates** zero-sections with fetch-failure, and there is **no per-drug log**, so **AMIODARONE's drop cause cannot be determined from the artifact.** Establishing it requires re-running the build with per-drug logging. Not inferred here.
  - **Moiety-synonym fragmentation (distinct sub-defect):** `ASPIRIN` and `ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID` are **two moiety keys for the same drug**; `ASPIRIN` carries only indications (34067-9) + dosage (34068-7) while `ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID` carries contraindications + interactions + warnings. Any lookup keyed on "aspirin" reaches the wrong record.
  - **Blast radius — RESEARCH ONLY (measured, not assumed).** Verify never reads this corpus; it resolves DailyMed **live** (`api/server.py:65`/`:460`/`:1206` → `api/data_sources/dailymed.py:103` → `_resolve_setids` `:137` → live `GET /v2/spls.json?drug_name=` `:141-142`). Research reads the corpus (`api/rag/retriever.py:22`/`:120`/`:189`/`:618`).
  - **Why [P2] (priority reasoning):** **not [P1]** — Research *degrades to other sources* rather than asserting anything false, and the highest-risk surface (Verify: structured drug input, explicit interaction claims) is **structurally immune**. **Not [P3]** — it is an honesty/credibility exposure on a **shipped, user-visible source claim**. **CROSS-REF the existing `[P2]` full-site DailyMed over-claim sweep — same honesty family:** we advertise DailyMed as the 5th Research retrieval source while its common-drug safety coverage has a hole we have never measured or disclosed. Same bar, adjacent entry.
  - **Sub-items:** (a) per-drug build logging to attribute the 705; (b) moiety-synonym normalization (ASPIRIN↔ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID); (c) decide whether Research needs Verify's openFDA fallback (own entry below); (d) publish/track the coverage number.
  - **🆕 2026-08-05 — sub-defect (b) above was RECORDED HERE ON 2026-07-27 AND THEN NOT APPLIED for five batons.** It is the direct cause of a false claim in BACKLOG and of a mis-attributed defect mechanism. See the adjacent entry **[P2 · adjudication method / corpus dual-key]** — filed separately because the *corpus* defect (this entry) and the *method* defect (adjudications that never consult it) have different fixes.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · label drift — surfaced 2026-08-06] `local` renders as "Local" on the server and "FDA" in the frontend map — same stored citation, two labels, and one of them is the chip c1 shipped to remove**
  - 📋 **RE-TAGGED `[DONE]` → `[HONESTY]` 2026-08-18, founder-ratified — the tag contradicted the entry's own body.** The body's closing line reads *"Only the LABEL half (`local` → 'FDA') remains open"*, and the 2026-08-18 recon confirmed it at HEAD `b6bfca7`: `share_renderer.py:206` still maps `"local"` → `"Local"` while `sourceLabels.ts` still maps `local` → `'FDA'` (the host-map half IS resolved on both sides). **A `[DONE]` tag on a half-open entry fed the nav-table class counts a wrong number and hid the open half from the `[HONESTY]` listing.** Class chosen per content: a user-visible label asserting "FDA" for a non-FDA-attributable stub on the residual path is an honesty/drift item. Rating unchanged `[P2]`; status unchanged (open at residual-accepted, live-session-only). **The `[DONE]` period (2026-08-06 → 2026-08-18) is recorded here rather than erased, so the tag history is auditable.**
  - **What:** `api/services/share_renderer.py:203` maps `local` → **"Local"**; `utils/sourceLabels.ts:68` maps `local` → **"FDA"** (documented there as a deliberate legacy fallback, since cached FDA labeling merges into the FDA chip).
  - ⚠️ **CURRENTLY UNREACHABLE for the #7 rows — verified by trace 2026-08-06, not assumed.** `/q/` and `/explore` are **FastAPI-rendered** (`api/server.py:2483`, `:2676`) with **no Next.js page**; `sourceLabels.ts` is consumed only by `components/CitationPanel.tsx`, which is rendered only by `pages/research.tsx` — i.e. **live-session answers**. `pages/history.tsx:337` passes `citations={[]}`, so history renders none. Since `enable_local=False`, live retrieval cannot produce a `local` citation. **The two surfaces never render the same stored row.**
  - **The residual:** a **stale client** holding a pre-c1 answer in an open session would render "FDA" on an empty stub — the exact chip c1 removed.
  - **Frontend suppression is the defence-in-depth fix — NOT BUILT** (founder decision 2026-08-07: `share_renderer` only for the #7 baton). It fixes this narrower residual, not #7.
  - **🟡 RESIDUAL ACCEPTED — founder decision 2026-08-10: the frontend mirror is NOT being built.** The residual is a **stale client holding a pre-c1 answer in a single live session tab** — `CitationPanel` via `pages/research.tsx:727` only, **never persisted, never public, visible to one user in one tab**. ⚠️ Cost of mirroring was a factor: `share_renderer` ships **2 locales** (`resolve_locale`, `share_renderer.py:390-398` — *"only en + zh-TW … Anything else → en"*), but the frontend ships **16** (`utils/i18n-ui.ts`), so **CLAUDE.md Rule 16 would require 16 translations** for a path that cannot be reached in practice. **The entry stays OPEN at residual-accepted status** — the residual shrinks further if **tombstone-at-write** ships (candidate D), which would close every persisted route. 🆕 **2026-08-10 — TOMBSTONE-AT-WRITE SHIPPED (`d85e85e`, fly 218): the PERSISTED-ROUTE residual is now CLOSED.** No new share can store an untombstoned `local` citation. **The remaining residual is LIVE-SESSION ONLY** — a stale client rendering a pre-c1 answer in its own tab via `CitationPanel` (`pages/research.tsx:727`), never persisted, never public — exactly as recorded 2026-08-10. **Entry stays open at residual-accepted; the residual is now strictly smaller.**
  - ❌ ~~**Note:** the separate **host-map** drift (`sourceLabels.ts:79` `dailymed` vs `share_renderer.py:244`) does **not** bear on this — all 8 stubs have `url:''`, so no host lookup occurs.~~
  - **✅ HOST-MAP HALF: RESOLVED — verified 2026-08-10, on BOTH sides.** `utils/sourceLabels.ts:101` → `if (host.includes('dailymed.nlm.nih.gov')) return 'dailymed';   // was 'rxnorm' …` and `api/services/share_renderer.py:246` → `("dailymed.nlm.nih.gov", "dailymed"),   # … (was 'rxnorm', debt-(2) drift)`. **Both map to `dailymed`; both carry a "was 'rxnorm'" comment — the fix landed on both surfaces.** ⚠️ **The line refs above were stale: `:79`→`:101` (`:79` is `ema: { label: 'EMA' }`) and `:244`→`:246`.** It also never bore on the #7 rows regardless — all 8 stubs have `url:''`, so no host lookup occurs. **Only the LABEL half (`local` → "FDA") remains open.**

- [OTHER] **[P2 · observability / PRODUCT code — surfaced 2026-08-06] The DailyMed rewrite arm is not observable at runtime: the queries that produce the ENTIRE DailyMed pool are logged nowhere, and the log line that looks like them is a different set**
  - **What:** `_dailymed_union_queries` (`api/rag/retriever.py:184`, calling `_rewrite_query` at `:404`) emits the strings that are actually searched against the DailyMed store — **the whole DailyMed half of Research retrieval** — and logs **nothing**. No INFO, no DEBUG, no capture hook, no debug flag.
  - **🔴 WORSE THAN ABSENT — MISLEADING.** `retriever.py:177-180` logs `Query rewritten: '<query>'` at INFO. That line is (a) a **different, independently-sampled set** — the K=1 rewrites used for PubMed/FDA/TFDA, not the K=3 DailyMed union — and (b) it logs the **INPUT, not the output**. The per-string outputs go to `logger.debug` (`:180`), and `api/server.py:11` pins `basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)`, **so DEBUG never emits at all.** A reader of the logs will believe they are seeing the DailyMed queries and be wrong on both counts.
  - **MEASURED CONSEQUENCE, not hypothetical:** the strings behind a **live production wrong-drug citation reproduced 12/12 across two index configurations** are **permanently unrecoverable**. Confirmed against the actual run log `tests/results/_c2_ab.log`, which shows the three rewrite POSTs firing per run with their outputs discarded. Closing the mechanism question required **re-sampling the distribution** (10 calls, [`docs/rewrite_arm_mechanism_20260806.md`](docs/rewrite_arm_mechanism_20260806.md)) because reconstruction was impossible.
  - **This is an observability gap in PRODUCT code — distinct from the instrument-blind class.** Those are measurement harnesses that mislead; this is shipped code that cannot be observed, and it made a *correct* harness insufficient.
  - **NO FIX PROPOSED and NO LOGGING CHANGED.** Noted only that a `logger.debug` on the union set would have sufficed — and that with `level=logging.INFO` in force, DEBUG alone would still not have emitted.
  - **Priority [P2]:** no user-facing harm; it costs measurement, and this line has spent six batons on measurement.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · adjudication method / corpus dual-key — surfaced 2026-08-05 by the T1 ownership assertion] Every moiety-keyed adjudication in this repo can have a per-drug blind spot, because one substance can occupy TWO `moiety` keys — and this was already recorded, and still missed, across five c2 batons**
  - **➡️ PROMOTED TO A RULE 2026-08-06 — CLAUDE.md Rule 23** (*"a lookup keyed on ONE identifier string is not a lookup on the entity — enumerate every key"*). Rule 23 carries the CHECK: **any per-drug conclusion states the full key set it used; a conclusion citing one key is unverified**, and owner / expected-drug fields are **plural by construction**. ⚠️ **The rule does not close this entry** — it addresses the *method*; the corpus still stores one substance under two keys, and the **prevalence remains UNMEASURED** (see below).
  - **What:** the DailyMed corpus key is the TFDA backbone `drug_name` **verbatim** (`scripts/build_dailymed_label_corpus.py:99-105`), with `_normalize_moiety()` used for **resolution only, never as the key**. So the same substance can be stored under two keys with **different reference labels and different safety coverage**:
    - `ASPIRIN` → **VAZALORE** (OTC) — **no** whitelisted safety sections
    - `ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID` → **DURLAZA** (Rx) — **34070-3 / 34073-7 / 43685-7**
  - **The concrete damage:** an adjudication that looked up only `ASPIRIN` concluded *"the queried drug's own reference label has no safety section"* and recorded it in **BACKLOG.md:860 as "6 of 6"**. That is **false for aspirin**: `DailyMed:4c2a1403-3862-1efd-0a91-444989222b37#34070-3` (*DURLAZA — Contraindications*) is **row 57 of the shipped index, 375 chars, embedding norm 0.9995, row-aligned** with `label_emb.npy (4608, 1536)`. The wrong-drug citation was mis-attributed to **coverage** when the corpus evidence points to **ranking**. Now re-pointed in BACKLOG:860 and in [`docs/c2_line_closeout_20260804.md`](docs/c2_line_closeout_20260804.md) §1.
  - **🔴 Why this is a METHOD defect and not just a data defect:** the dual-key fact was **already written in the entry immediately above (line 264) on 2026-07-27** — *"Any lookup keyed on 'aspirin' reaches the wrong record"* — and the c1 and c2 adjudications were nonetheless carried out single-key, for five batons, without consulting it. **The knowledge existed; the instrument did not consult it.** That is the *"instrument blind to its own target"* family (12th recorded instance) with a new twist: the blindness was **already documented**, so writing it down was not sufficient to prevent it. **A fix that is only a TECH_DEBT line will not prevent recurrence** — it has to be in whatever performs the lookup.
  - **⚠️ PREVALENCE IS UNMEASURED, DELIBERATELY (Rule 18).** 1038 moiety keys; **128 (12.3%) have no whitelisted safety section** — that number IS measured. How many of those 128 are shadowed by a synonym key that *does* carry safety sections is **NOT measured and must not be estimated.** The T1 baton's substring sweep returned `GLYCERIN→NITROGLYCERIN`, `PSEUDOEPHEDRINE→EPHEDRINE`, `LORATADINE→DESLORATADINE`, `SALICYLIC ACID→ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID` — all **different substances** — and its number was **withheld as untrustworthy**, correctly. A real census needs **RxNorm ingredient IDs**, i.e. a normalised vocabulary; substring matching is the same mention-style reasoning the ownership work exists to reject. **Out of scope; do not attempt it opportunistically.**
  - **Only 2 cases are hand-verified:** `ASPIRIN`/`ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID` (medically unambiguous — same substance) and `IBUPROFEN`/`IBUPROFEN LYSINE` (**same active moiety, but NEOPROFEN is an IV neonatal PDA product** — a clinical judgment, founder-pending as of this entry). **The other 4 of the 6 c2-adjudicated cases are NOT re-adjudicated.**
  - **🔴 THE DEFECT NOW HAS THREE MECHANISMS, WHERE THE RECORD SAYS TWO.** `docs/c2_line_closeout_20260804.md` records coverage **vs** ranking. Add **threshold**:
    - **coverage** — no owned safety document exists in the corpus (what c2 addresses)
    - **threshold** — an owned document exists **but never clears the store `min_score`**, so it cannot enter the pool at all
    - **ranking** — an owned document enters the pool and a wrong one out-competes it
  - ❌ ~~**❓ UNKNOWN, and it decides which mechanism is operating for aspirin:** whether *DURLAZA — Contraindications* clears the store `min_score`… **NOT verified**…~~ → **MEASURED 2026-08-06 (one embedding call): [`docs/durlaza_minscore_20260806.md`](docs/durlaza_minscore_20260806.md). It did NOT close the question — it reframed it.**
    - **On the deterministic RAW arm:** *DURLAZA — Contraindications* scores **0.4031** vs the **0.6** floor (margin −0.1969, unambiguous FAIL), at **in-store rank 500 / 4608**.
    - 🔴 **But `0 / 4608` documents clear 0.6 on the raw query — including `Clanza (Aceclofenac)` itself, at 0.5787 (rank 0, margin −0.0213, *at the ambiguity band edge*), which IS cited 12/12.** A document that cannot enter the pool via this arm is cited every time, so **the DailyMed pool on this query is produced entirely by REWRITTEN queries** (`_dailymed_union_queries`, `retriever.py:395-413`, ~9 rewrites + the raw augment). **The raw arm explains none of the observed behaviour.**
    - ❌ ~~**Verdict: PARTIAL.** … ❓ **Threshold vs ranking on the REWRITTEN arms remains UNKNOWN**…~~ → ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-06 — the rewritten arms were measured: [`docs/rewrite_arm_mechanism_20260806.md`](docs/rewrite_arm_mechanism_20260806.md).** 3 draws of `_dailymed_union_queries(k=3)` → **9 unique rewrite strings**, all embedded in one batched call (10 successful calls, at cap; no fallback fired).
    - **🔴 THE MECHANISM IS THRESHOLD, UNANIMOUSLY — 9/9 strings.** `DURLAZA — Contraindications` clears the 0.6 floor on **0 of 9** strings; best score **0.4051** (a **0.195** shortfall, not a near miss), in-store rank **632–2151** of 4608. It is **never a ranking contest** — the owned document never enters the pool. Also therefore **beyond reach of the safety-section whitelist exemption** (`retriever.py:38-48`), which only re-adds documents that were retrieved.
    - **ACECLOFENAC clears on 3/9** (0.6493–0.6787, rank 0) — and on **6/9 strings nothing clears at all**. **Surfaces BOTH: 0/9. Surfaces NEITHER: 6/9.**
    - **Cross-draw stability HIGH:** 6 of 9 strings appeared in all three draws; draws 2 and 3 were identical. The picture did not differ by draw.
    - ⚠️ **FRESH SAMPLE, not a reconstruction** — the historical 12/12 strings are **permanently unrecoverable** (see the observability-gap entry below).
    - **🔬 ROOT CAUSE, verified offline:** `scripts/build_dailymed_label_corpus.py:528` — **`texts = [d["content"] for d in docs]`**, so **only section content is embedded and the title (the only place the `moiety` appears) is NOT.** DURLAZA's contraindications text names the **brand** and never `aspirin`/`acetylsalicylic acid`; the ACECLOFENAC text **does** contain *"acetylsalicylic acid"* (cross-sensitivity). **Aspirin's own safety document is semantically invisible to aspirin's own name.** 🔴 **And the synonym key makes it worse:** the 3 strings containing *"acetylsalicylic acid"* — exactly what Rule 23 says to enumerate — are the only ones to push anything over the floor, and they push **ACECLOFENAC** (0.586 → **0.679**) while pushing DURLAZA to its **lowest** scores of the sample. ❌ ~~**UNMEASURED, not attempted, not recommended:** whether re-embedding content with the title prefixed would lift it over the floor~~ → **MEASURED OFFLINE 2026-08-06 on 11 documents / 2 drug cases, no corpus re-embed: [`docs/title_prefix_hypothesis_20260806.md`](docs/title_prefix_hypothesis_20260806.md). 🛑 THE HYPOTHESIS IS DEAD — DURLAZA clears 0.6 on 0/9 strings WITH the title too** (best **0.5614**, a 0.039 shortfall). 🔴 **And it is worse than neutral: it drops ACECLOFENAC below the floor on the only 3 strings where anything cleared, so the store would return NOTHING on all 9 — a wrong citation becomes ZERO citations.** ⚠️ **Yet it has real discriminating power in the right direction** — DURLAZA **+0.111** mean, ACECLOFENAC **−0.103** mean (**+0.215** differential), reversing the ordering on **5/9** strings, with its biggest gains (+0.18) exactly on the `acetylsalicylic acid` strings. **So the binding constraint is the 0.6 floor, not the embedded text** — every document sampled sits between 0.42 and 0.68; changing what is embedded reorders that band without lifting anything through it. *(NOT a recommendation to change the threshold — `local_threshold` is shared with the local and TFDA stores at `server.py:450`.)* **A corpus-wide change remains 🔴** (own §2.7 + section-aware danger-path re-gate + canary + prod human-eye gate), **blast radius = every Research query** (all 4608 docs re-embedded, not a countable subset), and **the $0.063 re-embed is not the cost — gate cycles and blast radius are.** **Corpus-wide effect remains UNMEASURED; 2 drug cases is evidence for a decision, not a measurement.**
    - 🆕 **Incidental, one query only:** Lever 1's *"deterministic raw augment"* (`retriever.py:398-399`, shipped `1217d66`) contributes **zero** documents on this query.
  - ❌ ~~**🔴 THE FILTER PREMISE IS STILL UNVERIFIED (open-item #2).**~~ → 🔴 **MEASURED 2026-08-06, and the premise does NOT hold on this query.** Denominators named: removing ACECLOFENAC yields a **CORRECT** citation on **0 / 9** unique rewrite strings sampled; it yields **ZERO** DailyMed citations on **3 / 3** of the strings where ACECLOFENAC is actually surfaced — i.e. **every string on which the filter would fire at all.** On the other 6/9 the filter would do nothing, because nothing clears the floor. ⚠️ **One query, one fresh sample — NOT a recommendation for or against the filter, which remains a founder decision.**
  - **⚠️ TWO GATES, NOT ONE** (`retriever.py:628-632`): the cosine floor `min_score=0.6` (`local_threshold`, `api/server.py:450`) **and** an in-store top-N cutoff `n_results = max_results = 5` (`vector_store.py:120-121`). So the mechanism split is **FOUR-way — coverage · threshold · in-store rank cutoff · downstream ranking** — where this file previously recorded two and `docs/c2_line_closeout_20260804.md` recorded coverage-vs-ranking. **🟡 FLAGGED, NOT ACTED ON:** whether the in-store `n_results=5` cutoff and the **[P2] Research pool budget `top_k=5`** entry are the same budget question filed twice is a **sequencing call for the founder**, not settled here.
  - **⚠️ WHY THE UNKNOWN MATTERS — it changes what a wrong-drug filter DOES.** If DURLAZA never clears threshold, then a filter that removes ACECLOFENAC turns this query from **wrong citation** into **zero citations**, not into a correct one. A filter is the right fix for the *ranking* mechanism and the wrong fix for the *threshold* one. **This should be measured before STATE open-item #2 is built, not after.**
  - **Priority reasoning — [P2]:** no new user-visible harm beyond what the existing entries already carry, but it **silently corrupts measurement**, and this line has now spent five batons on measurement. **Not [P1]** because nothing ships from it directly; **not [P3]** because every future moiety-keyed adjudication inherits it.
  - **Fix directions (none chosen — founder's call):** (i) a shared owner-resolution helper that every probe/adjudication calls, so single-key lookups stop being written by hand — `tests/probes/wrongdrug/owner_assertion.py` is the existing candidate, it already takes a **set** of owner moieties; (ii) normalise the corpus key at build time (an `api/`+rebuild change, and it would alter `source_id`s); (iii) leave the corpus alone and require every adjudication to state its owner set explicitly.
  - **🔎 2026-07-29 REFRAME — 93.8% OF THE NO-SAFETY CLASS IS A FIXABLE *SELECTION* ARTIFACT, NOT MISSING DATA. This is the c2 scope.**
    - **120 of the 128 no-safety moieties (93.8%)** carry exactly `{34067-9 indications, 34068-7 dosage}` — the **OTC Drug-Facts shape**. The reference label selected for them is an **NDA-approved OTC brand**: **Aleve · ADVIL · VAZALORE · Prilosec OTC · PEPCID AC · Tagamet · Lamisil AT · Nasacort**. The build's `marketing_category_code NDA > NDA_AG > ANDA > other` preference has **no Rx-vs-OTC tiebreak**, so it *actively prefers* OTC brand NDAs over Rx generic ANDAs — and **OTC Drug Facts labels carry no safety LOINC sections at all.**
    - This **supersedes the "ASPIRIN + NAPROXEN ⚠️ genuine content gap" line above as a general characterization**: naproxen is not a content gap, it is a *selection* outcome. For the same moieties openFDA's hit is an **Rx label with real interactions text** (Naproxen **10,898** · Terbinafine 3,570 · Famotidine 1,428 chars). ⚠️ **Inference from the openFDA mirror — NOT confirmed by a live DailyMed query. Confirming that is c2's prerequisite.**
    - The **23 drugs absent entirely** (biologics / insulins / GLP-1s / controlled substances) are the corpus's **declared TFDA-mono-moiety scope boundary**, consistent with the INSULIN line above — **founder scope call**, not a build tweak.
  - **🔴 2026-07-29 — THIS IS A PARTIAL *UPSTREAM* FIX FOR THE WRONG-DRUG CITATION DEFECT. Mapped case by case, 5 for 5:**
    | queried drug | its DailyMed reference label | its safety sections | wrongly cited | that label | c2-fixable? |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | **aspirin** 🔴 | VAZALORE (**OTC**) | **NONE** | ACECLOFENAC | Clanza (Rx), 2 sections | ⚠️ **probably NOT** — mono-aspirin looks OTC-only (openFDA also lands on OTC) |
    | **omeprazole** 🔴 | Prilosec **OTC** | **NONE** | PANTOPRAZOLE SODIUM | Protonix (Rx), 3 sections | ✅ YES |
    | **ibuprofen** 🟠 | ADVIL (**OTC**) | **NONE** | PIROXICAM · MEFENAMIC ACID | Rx generics, all 4 | ✅ YES |
    | **cimetidine** 🟡 | Tagamet (**OTC**) | **NONE** | COBIMETINIB | Cotellic (Rx), 3 sections | ✅ YES |
    | **naproxen** | Aleve (**OTC**) | **NONE** | NABUMETONE | Rx generic, 3 sections | ✅ YES |
    - **In every measured instance the queried drug's own label has NO safety section and the wrongly-cited sibling's DOES.** The retriever is not choosing badly among comparable options — it has nothing on-target and reaches for the nearest thing carrying a safety section, which is always an Rx label.
    - **⚠️ CORRECTION to a stated premise:** omeprazole→PANTOPRAZOLE had been read as *not* coverage-caused because "PPIs measured REDUNDANT". The four PPIs measured REDUNDANT are **esomeprazole, pantoprazole, lansoprazole, rabeprazole — the siblings. Omeprazole itself is `no_safety`** (reference label = Prilosec OTC, zero safety sections). So it **is** coverage-caused, and it is the cleanest illustration of the mechanism: the substituted label belongs to a REDUNDANT sibling.
    - **🔴 CONCLUSION — the wrong-drug defect has TWO causes: (1) coverage** (dominant in every measured instance; c2-fixable) **and (2) ranking/precision** (demonstrated by `POTASSIUM CHLORIDE #34073-7` correct-but-cited-1/8 vs `POTASSIUM ACETATE #34070-3` wrong-but-cited-4/8 with **both** in the pool). **➡️ THE PENDING WRONG-DRUG BUILD BATON MUST RE-TAKE ITS PRE-FIX BASELINE *AFTER* c2, NOT BEFORE** — a baseline taken now measures a population dominated by cause (1), which c2 is expected to remove. **This constrains when the baseline is valid; it does NOT reorder the baton — the founder sequences.**
    - **⚠️ ESCALATED 2026-07-30 — "re-baseline" was TOO WEAK. The pending wrong-drug FILTER build must be RE-EVALUATED FOR NECESSITY after c2, not merely re-measured.** The adjudication is **5 for 5 coverage-caused** (aspirin the likely exception, and even it is a coverage question — whether an Rx mono-aspirin SPL exists at all). If c2 gives the retriever an on-target safety section for naproxen / omeprazole / ibuprofen / cimetidine, the *reason* the wrong sibling was returned disappears for those cases. **c2 may resolve most or all of the defect.** The residual would be cause (2) alone — one measured instance (spironolactone), on a query where the correct document *was* in the pool.
      **➡️ RECOMMENDATION: after c2 ships, re-adjudicate the six cases BEFORE building any citation-integrity filter. The build may descope substantially or close outright.** Building a filter first risks adding a permanent runtime guard against a defect a data fix removes — and every filter is a precision/recall tradeoff on the danger path. **Founder sequences; this records the dependency, not a reordering.**
    - **📉 c1 SIDE-EFFECT THAT RAISES c2's URGENCY (recorded 2026-07-30):** deprecating local **thins OTC-class pools** — `aspirin contraindications` went from **2.7 to 1.0 documents/run** (a single DailyMed Contraindications section in all 3 runs), ibuprofen 4.7→2.0, naproxen 5.0→4.0. **This is CORRECT and was the right call** — the removed documents were empty, and honest thinness beats fake fullness. **But it stops papering over the OTC coverage hole and makes it visible**: these answers now visibly stand on 1–2 documents, where before the count was padded by stubs carrying one character. **c1 did not create the hole; it uncovered it. c2 is what fills it.**
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-27. **Reframed + wrong-drug mapping:** 2026-07-29. **Evidence:** `docs/pair_aware_retrieval_probe.md` §7 · [`docs/local_corpus_decision_20260729.md`](docs/local_corpus_decision_20260729.md) §1 · [`docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md`](docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md) Task 4.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · retrieval honesty / mitigation-not-carried-across — surfaced by the pair-aware probe 2026-07-27] Verify falls back to openFDA when a DailyMed label lacks the 34073-7 interactions section; RESEARCH HAS NO SUCH FALLBACK — the same known gap is mitigated on one surface and unmitigated on the other**
  - **What:** `api/server.py:1202-1204` documents the gap **and names aspirin explicitly**: *"A label counts here ONLY if its LOINC 34073-7 Drug Interactions section is present (OTC Drug-Facts labels — e.g. aspirin — lack it → treated as a miss so we fall through to openFDA)."* Verify therefore degrades gracefully. **Research's DailyMed path has no equivalent** — an OTC Drug-Facts drug simply contributes no safety section to the pool, silently.
  - **⚠️ SCOPE NUANCE (do not over-state this):** the pair-aware probe found interaction content is **bilateral** — a counterpart drug's label documents the pair — so **PAIR queries are largely unaffected** (measured: ANY-drug reach 8/8 on 7 of 10 canonical pair queries, including `warfarin+aspirin` at 4/8 via WARFARIN's own section). **The real exposure is SINGLE-DRUG safety queries about an OTC Drug-Facts drug** (e.g. "aspirin contraindications"), where no counterpart label exists to carry the content.
  - **CLASS OF BUG — a mitigation existed on one surface and was not carried across when the same data source was extended to a second surface.** Structurally identical to b1's *"country-keyed the DATA but not the STRINGS"* (2026-07-27, `docs/locale_waterfall_b1_build.md` §5): the extension moved the data path and left the compensating logic behind. **See the proposed CLAUDE.md rule in the ship record — founder to ratify.**
  - **Fix direction (not scoped):** either give Research the same openFDA fall-through for whitelisted-safety-section misses, or accept it explicitly and record why. **Do not silently rely on bilateral coverage** — it does not hold for single-drug queries.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-27. **Evidence:** `docs/pair_aware_retrieval_probe.md` §7.3.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · link-health cron — surfaced by the 在地差異 b1 build 2026-07-26] `moh.gov.my` returns 403 to every automated check (WAF bot-block) → the PRD §5.1.1 monthly `url_native` HEAD-check cron will report it dead FOREVER unless it ships with an allowlist exception**
  - **What:** `MY_MOH.url_native = https://www.moh.gov.my/` (`utils/localeHint.ts`) is the official Malaysia MOH domain and the structural sibling of the HTTP-200-confirmed `moh.gov.sg`, but it **403s both `curl` (browser UA) and WebFetch** — a WAF bot-block, not a dead site. It is **BROWSER-CONFIRMED** live by the founder at b1 prod-gate row 2 (2026-07-26); it is **TOOL-UNCONFIRMED** and will stay that way.
  - **Why it's debt (the cron doesn't exist yet — this is a note for when it's built):** PRD §5.1.1 specifies a **monthly cron doing HEAD checks on every `url_native`**. A naive implementation flags `moh.gov.my` broken on every single run. One permanently-crying URL is worse than no monitor: the alert gets muted, and then a *genuinely* dead authority link (the exact failure the cron exists to catch — a regulator reorganising its domain, which is the normal way these links rot) goes unnoticed inside the noise. Link-health for the 在地差異 panel is a **credibility** surface: the panel's entire claim is "here is the official authority to verify against".
  - **Fix (when the cron is built, not before):** carry a per-URL `bot_blocked: true` / allowlist flag in the authority data (or a cron-side exception list) that maps 403-from-a-known-WAF to a distinct **"unverifiable-by-robot, human-recheck quarterly"** state, NOT to "dead". Do **not** silently treat all 403s as OK — that would hide real 403-serving breakage on other URLs.
  - **Related:** inline comment at the `MY_MOH` data entry in `utils/localeHint.ts` records the same TOOL-UNCONFIRMED / BROWSER-CONFIRMED status.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-26 (b1 pre-deploy edits).

- [DONE] **[P3 · deploy tooling — surfaced during the fly-211 deploy 2026-07-24; ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-03 — close condition MET on the fly-244 deploy, transcript `tests/probes/deploy_parser/fly244_deploy_transcript.txt`] `deploy.ps1` stopped-machine parser (`^\S+`) skips fly's indented `fly status` rows → the auto-restart step is a no-op; the script claims a step it does not perform**
  - **What:** `deploy.ps1` Step 3 extracts stopped-machine IDs with `if ($_ -match "^\S+\s+(\S+)")` (line ~22), but `fly status` rows begin with **leading whitespace** (` app │ …`), so `^\S+` never matches → `$stopped` is always empty → the script prints "All machines running" and **skips Steps 4–6 (start stopped machines)** even when a machine is `stopped`.
  - **Observed (fly 211):** machine `2879720c66d478` was `stopped` (autostop baseline, on v211, fly "good state"); the script reported "All machines running" without restarting it. **Harmless** because Fly autostop wakes the machine on the first inbound request, so prod is reachable regardless.
  - **Why it's still debt:** the script's stated purpose ("auto-restart any stopped machines after deployment", per CLAUDE.md) is **not actually performed** — a Rule-18 "claims a step it doesn't do" mismatch. If a machine is ever stopped for a NON-autostart reason (crash-loop, OOM), the script would silently leave it down while reporting success.
  - **Fix (small):** parse the machine ID from the whitespace-prefixed row (e.g. `-match "\bapp\b\s+(\S+)"` or split on `│`), or use `fly machines list --json` and filter `state == "stopped"`. Behavior with autostop is unchanged; the fix restores the honest auto-restart for the non-autostart case.
  - **📌 2026-08-26 RECURRED (fly 240 deploy) + mechanism verified EMPIRICALLY, not re-inferred:** Step 3 printed "All machines running." while its own echoed table showed `2879720c66d478` `stopped`. Ran the script's exact pipeline against live `fly status` in the same shell: `\bstopped\b` **matches 1 row** (detection is fine), the row's **first codepoint is 32** (leading space → `^\S+` fails → the ForEach yields nothing → `$stopped` empty), and even absent the leading space `(\S+)` would capture the `│` box char (codepoint 9474), not the machine ID — so this entry and the older "box-char false-negative" entry below are the SAME defect, two symptoms. **This time the machine did not wake on traffic during post-deploy `/health` polls (fly-proxy served the started machine), so it was started manually — `flyctl machine start 2879720c66d478` — per the script's stated intent; both machines then `started` on v240.** [P3] unchanged; founder to re-rate if the manual step recurring per deploy changes the calculus.
  - **📌 2026-09-01 RECURRED AGAIN (fly 241 deploy) — 2nd recurrence since filing.** Step 3's own echoed table showed `2879720c66d478` `stopped` at v241 directly above the script's "All machines running." line; started manually with `flyctl machine start 2879720c66d478`, both machines then `started` at v241. Same mechanism, nothing new to diagnose — the entry's small fix is now three deploys overdue.
  - **📌 2026-09-02 RECURRED AGAIN (fly 242 deploy, HISTORY-car segment 1) — 3rd recurrence.** Identical signature: `2879720c66d478` `stopped` at v242 in the script's own echoed table, "All machines running." printed below it; started manually (`flyctl machine start 2879720c66d478`), both machines then `started` on the v242 image. **The small fix is now four deploys overdue** — same manual step every deploy; founder re-rate standing offer from the 2026-08-26 line stands.
  - **📌 2026-09-02 RECURRED AGAIN, same day (fly 243 deploy, HISTORY-car delete segment) — 4TH RECURRENCE.** Same machine, same signature: `2879720c66d478` `stopped` at v243 in the script's echoed table, "All machines running." printed below it; started manually, both machines `started` on the v243 image (`deployment-01M1GDTBKSB9498W8WNE4Y4DV3`). **The small fix is now FIVE deploys overdue.**
  - **🔧 FIX APPLIED 2026-09-02 (commit `679c841`) — ENTRY STAYS OPEN until the close condition.** Step 3 now extracts the machine ID as the row's only **14-hex token** (`\b([0-9a-f]{14})\b`), layout-independent — chosen over this entry's suggested `\bapp\b\s+(\S+)` because, per this entry's own 2026-08-26 empirics, that form would itself capture the box char (9474), not the ID. **Verified OFFLINE per Rule 20:** `tests/probes/deploy_parser/parse_stopped_probe.ps1` + committed result JSON — the OLD pipeline yields **0 IDs** against a reconstructed failing-shape row (sample provenance stated in the probe header: rebuilt from this entry's recorded codepoint facts 32/9474, NOT a byte-capture — none was retained), the FIXED pipeline yields `2879720c66d478`, the legacy pre-box-char row shape is also detected (flyctl auto-upgrades; format has drifted once), and an all-`started` sample yields nothing. **CLOSE CONDITION: no recurrence at the next real deploy** — either "Found stopped machines:" prints and the machine is started by the script, or the echoed table genuinely shows all `started`. *(Side find, fixed in the same commit's probe iteration: PS 5.1 reads BOM-less UTF-8 scripts as ANSI and the mojibake can swallow a code line — deploy.ps1's new comment and the probe are kept pure ASCII for that reason.)*
  - **🎯 CLOSE-CONDITION TEST = the fly-244 deploy (HISTORY car segment 3, 2026-09-03).** Pre-deploy baseline `fly status`: `2879720c66d478` `stopped` at v243 (autostop), `683d447c2e5428` `started` — so Step 3 WILL meet a stopped machine. Full stdout/stderr of the run is captured to `tests/probes/deploy_parser/fly244_deploy_transcript.txt`; the outcome (parser branch taken, machine states, `/health` revision) is recorded below this line after the deploy.
  - **✅ CLOSE CONDITION MET 2026-09-03 (fly 244) — the fix worked on its first real deploy, no manual start.** Transcript (`tests/probes/deploy_parser/fly244_deploy_transcript.txt`, 67,558 bytes, full stdout/stderr of `deploy.ps1`, nothing redacted): Step 3's echoed table showed `2879720c66d478` `stopped` at v244 and the script printed **`Found stopped machines: 2879720c66d478`** (branch: DETECTED — not the "All machines running." no-op of recurrences 1–4); Step 4 printed `Starting 2879720c66d478 ...` / `2879720c66d478 has been started`; Step 6's final table showed BOTH `2879720c66d478` and `683d447c2e5428` v244 `started`, then "All machines running.", exit 0. Independent readbacks after the script: `fly status` both `started` at v244; `/health` `revision` = `b718a3d616a8ecee3b8e47aa00ecdc89b683f8d3` on the first poll = the pushed HEAD. ⚠️ Transcript-reading note: the `│` box characters appear as `??` in the file — mojibake from the capture pipe (powershell.exe → Git Bash `tee`), not from the script; the 14-hex-token regex is layout-independent, which is exactly why the capture encoding did not matter. **Closed by the fix commit `679c841` + this evidence; re-open only on a recurrence.**

  - **Masked, not fixed:** the fly-209 exemption (`65da848`, surface iii) re-adds dropped **whitelisted SAFETY sections** (interactions/contra/warnings/boxed) only. **Non-whitelisted relevance errors persist** — the filter can still drop a relevant PubMed study or a descriptive label section, or keep an off-topic study, on ANY Research query.

Do NOT bundle with a targeted exemption.

  - **▶️ 2026-08-26 — PROMOTED TO THE SUCCESSOR LINE of 主線 A #1 (founder Ruling B — `docs/batons/positioning_audit_20260823.md` §3):** with the reserved seat **PARKED premise-refuted**, this entry is the schedulable carrier of the line's open question, in both halves: the **judgment half** filed above (keeps off-topic, drops the drug's own safety section — masked only for whitelisted safety LOINCs) and the **content half** — *which 60% does the filter drop*: the 2026-08-23 attrition measurement is **counts-only** (`retriever.py:531` logs `N -> M`, never which documents), stated as the limitation row of `tests/probes/retrieval_attrition/README.md` — *"Nothing here says whether the filter dropped the **right** 60%."* No longer waiting on 主線-A sequencing; **scheduling itself remains the founder's**. The blast-radius caveat above stands (LLM-judgment change on EVERY Research query → full §2.7). Annotation only — class and [P2] rating unchanged.

- [OTHER] **[P1 · measurement-integrity — surfaced by the reranker probe 2026-07-16] `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` dataset is CENSORED — silently excludes the ~32% rerank-failed queries, non-randomly**
  - **What:** the shadow record is built only when `_sw_shadow_sink` is non-empty (`server.py:954`), and the sink is populated only via `_score_sink` (`retriever.py:199`), which the rerank-skip path leaves EMPTY (same root cause as the entry above). So every v198-onward `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` record **omits the queries where rerank skipped** — and it omits them **non-randomly**: exactly the **large-pool (≥6-doc)** queries, which are the ones most likely to contain a label-vs-study inversion the shadow exists to measure.
  - **Consequence:** any conclusion drawn from shadow data (e.g. "the composite rarely changes top-k", "×1.5 label weight is sufficient") is computed on a sample **biased toward small pools** and must carry this caveat until slice 2 lands. In particular the B-2 Phase-2b prominence result was measured on the surviving (rerank-succeeded) slice only.
  - **Resolution — DE-CENSORING NOW IN EFFECT as of fly 208 (2026-07-17, reranker slice 2 `4319884`):** the rerank-skip is fixed, so `_score_sink` now populates on the previously-failing ~32% (large-pool) queries and `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` records them for the first time. **⚠️ EXPECT the shadow's volume + composition to SHIFT** (more large pools, more label-vs-study inversions surfacing) — this is the sample **de-censoring**, NOT a regression. Do NOT read the pre-208 vs post-208 shadow delta as a behavior change. **Closure LEFT TO THE FOUNDER** until enough post-fix shadow data accumulates to characterize the de-censored distribution. Cross-ref `tests/results/reranker_instability_probe_REPORT.md` + the reranker [P1] entry above.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-16 (probe). **De-censoring in effect:** 2026-07-17 (fly 208).

- [OTHER] **[P2 · lever-verification method — surfaced v198, 2026-07-06] The lever-digest fingerprint is no longer unique across shadow flags**
  - **What:** `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` (added v198) and `RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` both resolve to Fly digest **`d8c5ac2e11c8e492` (= `d8c5ac2e`)** because Fly hashes the secret **VALUE** (`'true'`), NOT the flag name — identical values → identical digest.
  - **Consequence:** the long-standing "`RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` digest = `d8c5ac2e`" check can no longer be read as proof that RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL **alone** is unchanged — the same digest now covers two flags (and any future flag also set to `'true'` will collide too).
  - **FIX (method, not code):** every future lever-state verification must enumerate **EACH flag's state by name** (`RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW`, `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW`, C1 / `DIRECTION_CHECK_SHADOW`, Lever 2 / `QUESTION_NEUTRALIZATION_SHADOW`, locale-hint), not compare a single shared digest. Update the deploy / post-deploy checklist wording accordingly.
  - **Not a bug in v198** — a verification-method blind spot introduced by a second shadow flag sharing a value.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-06 (v198 review).
  - **🆕 SECOND-SURFACE wrinkle (fly 210, 2026-07-22 — locale-hint enable):** the `locale-hint` lever is **NOT a fly secret** — it is `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOCALE_HINT_ENABLED`, a **build-time build-arg** in committed `fly.toml [build.args]` (`= "true"`, commit `8499495`). It therefore **NEVER appears in `fly secrets list`** — its state is auditable ONLY in the committed `fly.toml` (and, at the bundle level, by whether `localeHintEnabled` constant-folds out of the research-chunk gate). **So future lever enumerations must check TWO surfaces:** (1) `fly secrets list` for the five secret-based flags (RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW / SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW / SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE / DIRECTION_CHECK_SHADOW / QUESTION_NEUTRALIZATION_SHADOW), AND (2) `fly.toml [build.args]` for `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOCALE_HINT_ENABLED`. Enumerating only `fly secrets list` would silently omit the locale-hint state entirely. Same class of blind spot as the shared-digest caveat above — a lever whose state lives on a surface the default check doesn't read.
